4.13.2 The four charts — GET /api/dashboard/charts?period=YYYY-MM

The 12-month window is the selected period and the eleven preceding months, generated as an axis in JavaScript from lib/datetime.js; each series is left-joined onto that axis. A month with no bill at all emits null, never 0, so Chart.js plots a gap in the line (D-14: a missing month is a gap, not zero).

revenue_collected needs one extra distinction, because "nothing collected yet" and "no such month" are different facts and the naive WHERE status='Paid' query collapses them into the same missing row. The query therefore drives off approved bills of the period and sums only the Paid ones, so a period with approved-but-unpaid bills emits 0.00 while a period with no bills emits no row at all and left-joins to null:

SELECT period,
       CAST(SUM(CASE WHEN status = 'Paid' THEN total ELSE 0 END) AS DECIMAL(12,2)) AS collected
  FROM bills
 WHERE period BETWEEN ? AND ?
   AND status IN ('Unpaid','Paid')          -- approved bills only; Draft is not a month
 GROUP BY period;

The axis join then maps a present row to its collected value ("0.00" when nothing is Paid) and an absent row to null. Against the D-44 seed no month is absent: the eleven history periods are fully Paid and the current period carries 66 approved bills, so the series ends "0.00" and contains no null.

KeyChartSQLStatuses counted
water_unitsLine, 12 months, หน่วยSELECT b.period, SUM(bi.quantity) FROM bill_items bi JOIN bills b ON b.id = bi.bill_id WHERE bi.item_type = 'water' AND b.period BETWEEN ? AND ? AND b.status IN ('Unpaid','Paid') GROUP BY b.periodApproved
electric_unitsLine, 12 months, หน่วยAs above with item_type = 'electric'Approved
revenue_collectedBar, 12 months, บาทThe CASE query above — grouped over approved bills, summing Paid ones onlyPaid only, by billing period; 0.00 when the period has approved bills but none Paid
status_countsDoughnut, selected periodSELECT SUM(status = 'Paid') AS paid, SUM(status = 'Unpaid') AS unpaid_or_overdue FROM bills WHERE period = ? AND status IN ('Unpaid','Paid')Approved; Draft excluded. The second slice is labelled รอชำระ + เกินกำหนด because the split between them is a display rule (D-21)

4.13.3 The four reports (D-42) — Admin only

Each is a page under /reports/… and each accepts ?format=csv, producing UTF-8 with a BOM through lib/csv.js with the filename pattern report-{name}-{period}.csv (D-42). No PDF export, no report builder, no ad-hoc column chooser.

#Thai titleRoute / CSV slugRange
1สรุปการเรียกเก็บและรับชำระรายเดือน/reports/monthly · monthly?from= / ?to= periods, default the last 12
2รายการค้างชำระ/reports/arrears · arrearsAll periods, as of CURDATE()
3ประวัติรายห้อง 12 เดือน/reports/room-history · room-historyOne room, 12 periods ending at ?period=
4ห้องที่ใช้น้ำ/ไฟสูงสุด/reports/top-usage · top-usageOne period, top 10, ?utility=water|electric

Report 1 — สรุปการเรียกเก็บและรับชำระรายเดือน. One row per period: billed, collected, outstanding, each split water / electricity / common fee, plus bill counts by status.

SELECT b.period,
       SUM(CASE WHEN b.status IN ('Unpaid','Paid') THEN b.total    ELSE 0 END) AS billed,
       SUM(CASE WHEN b.status =  'Paid'            THEN b.total    ELSE 0 END) AS collected,
       SUM(CASE WHEN b.status =  'Unpaid'          THEN b.total    ELSE 0 END) AS outstanding,
       SUM(CASE WHEN b.status IN ('Unpaid','Paid') THEN s.water    ELSE 0 END) AS water_billed,
       SUM(CASE WHEN b.status IN ('Unpaid','Paid') THEN s.electric ELSE 0 END) AS electric_billed,
       SUM(CASE WHEN b.status IN ('Unpaid','Paid') THEN s.common   ELSE 0 END) AS common_billed,
       SUM(b.status = 'Draft')  AS n_draft,
       SUM(b.status = 'Unpaid') AS n_unpaid,
       SUM(b.status = 'Paid')   AS n_paid
  FROM bills b
  LEFT JOIN (SELECT bill_id,                     -- one row per bill: the join must not fan out
                    SUM(CASE WHEN item_type = 'water'    THEN amount END) AS water,
                    SUM(CASE WHEN item_type = 'electric' THEN amount END) AS electric,
                    SUM(CASE WHEN item_type = 'common'   THEN amount END) AS common
               FROM bill_items GROUP BY bill_id) s ON s.bill_id = b.id
 WHERE b.period BETWEEN ? AND ?
 GROUP BY b.period
 ORDER BY b.period DESC;

Assertions: billed − collected = outstanding on every row, and water_billed + electric_billed + common_billed = billed on every row.

Report 2 — รายการค้างชำระ. Aged into the four non-overlapping buckets of D-42, sorted by days overdue descending.

SELECT b.room_code_snapshot, b.tenant_name_snapshot, t.phone, b.period,
       b.total, b.due_date,
       DATEDIFF(CURDATE(), b.due_date) AS days_overdue,
       CASE WHEN DATEDIFF(CURDATE(), b.due_date) <=  7 THEN '1-7'
            WHEN DATEDIFF(CURDATE(), b.due_date) <= 30 THEN '8-30'
            WHEN DATEDIFF(CURDATE(), b.due_date) <= 60 THEN '31-60'
            ELSE '61+' END AS bucket
  FROM bills b
  JOIN tenants t ON t.id = b.tenant_id
 WHERE b.status = 'Unpaid'                       -- stored status, D-21 is a display rule
   AND b.due_date < CURDATE()                    -- see the reconciliation note below
 ORDER BY days_overdue DESC, b.room_code_snapshot;
Reconciling D-42's two halves — recorded interpretation

D-42 says the report covers "every Unpaid bill across all periods" and that "the four bucket counts must sum to the row count". Those cannot both hold while a not-yet-due Unpaid bill is a row, because the buckets start at day 1. Resolution: the table rows are Unpaid bills with due_date < CURDATE() — exactly the bills displaying as เกินกำหนด — so the four buckets partition the rows and both assertions pass. Nothing becomes invisible: a summary line above the table reports the residual, ยังไม่ครบกำหนด {n} ใบ · {amount} บาท, and the all-time Dashboard tile arrears_all_time still counts every Unpaid bill. Bills whose payment was voided remain in the report — they are genuinely unpaid — and carry the badge ระงับการแจ้งเตือนอัตโนมัติ (D-25).

Report 3 — ประวัติรายห้อง 12 เดือน. The screen the office opens when a tenant disputes a reading.

SELECT b.period,
       MAX(CASE WHEN i.item_type = 'water'    THEN i.quantity END) AS water_units,
       MAX(CASE WHEN i.item_type = 'water'    THEN i.amount   END) AS water_amount,
       MAX(CASE WHEN i.item_type = 'electric' THEN i.quantity END) AS electric_units,
       MAX(CASE WHEN i.item_type = 'electric' THEN i.amount   END) AS electric_amount,
       MAX(CASE WHEN i.item_type = 'common'   THEN i.amount   END) AS common_amount,
       b.total,
       CASE WHEN b.status = 'Unpaid' AND b.due_date < CURDATE()
            THEN 'Overdue' ELSE b.status END AS display_status,
       MAX(p.paid_at) AS paid_at, MAX(r.receipt_no) AS receipt_no,
       MAX(p.voided_at) AS payment_voided_at        -- non-NULL renders the ยกเลิก stamp
  FROM bills b
  JOIN bill_items i ON i.bill_id = b.id
  -- the room's MOST RECENT payment, voided or not — FR-25 requires a voided
  -- payment to stay visible, so this may NOT be joined on active_bill_id
  LEFT JOIN payments p ON p.bill_id = b.id
                      AND p.id = (SELECT MAX(p2.id) FROM payments p2
                                   WHERE p2.bill_id = b.id)
  LEFT JOIN receipts r ON r.payment_id = p.id
 WHERE b.room_id = ? AND b.period BETWEEN ? AND ?
 GROUP BY b.id
 ORDER BY b.period DESC;

This report is scoped by room_id, so it deliberately spans tenancies: an outgoing tenant's bills, payments and receipts stay attached to that tenant and remain visible in the room's history (D-08). It is the only report that uses the display_status expression, because it is the only one whose status column a human reads row by row.

Why the payment join is not active_bill_id. FR-25 requires a voided payment to appear in the room's history stamped ยกเลิก, and this is the only surface that renders that history. A join on p.active_bill_id = b.id sees live payments only, so a voided payment and its voided receipt number would vanish: the bill would read เกินกำหนด with an empty payment date, and the office would lose the evidence that money was taken and returned. The row therefore renders receipt_no with the ยกเลิก stamp whenever payment_voided_at is non-NULL, and the bill's own display_status is unaffected — the void already returned it to Unpaid (D-25).

Report 4 — ห้องที่ใช้น้ำ/ไฟสูงสุด. Top 10 by units for the selected period, each with its own previous-month comparison.

SELECT b.room_code_snapshot, i.quantity AS units, i.amount,
       prev.quantity AS prev_units,
       i.quantity - prev.quantity AS delta_units
  FROM bills b
  JOIN bill_items i ON i.bill_id = b.id AND i.item_type = ?          -- 'water' | 'electric'
  LEFT JOIN bills pb ON pb.room_id = b.room_id AND pb.period = ?     -- the preceding period
                    AND pb.status IN ('Unpaid','Paid')
  LEFT JOIN bill_items prev ON prev.bill_id = pb.id AND prev.item_type = i.item_type
 WHERE b.period = ? AND b.status IN ('Unpaid','Paid')
 ORDER BY i.quantity DESC
 LIMIT 10;

A room with no bill in the preceding period yields NULL comparisons, rendered as —, never as a fabricated 0.

4.14 Error codes introduced by this section

All returned in the standard envelope with a stable UPPER_SNAKE code that tests assert on and a Thai message the user reads (ADR §6.3). Every code below also appears in the exhaustive catalogue of §3.1.6; the five the engine contributes to it are PERIOD_INVALID, RATE_NOT_FOUND, ROOM_NOT_BILLABLE, PAYMENT_NOT_FOUND and BILL_NOT_REVERTABLE.

codeStatusRaised byThai message
PERIOD_INVALID422Any endpoint taking periodรูปแบบรอบบิลไม่ถูกต้อง
READING_BELOW_PREVIOUS422POST /api/readings, replacement box offเลขมิเตอร์ปัจจุบันน้อยกว่าเดือนก่อน ({previous_reading}) กรุณาตรวจสอบ
REPLACEMENT_INVALID422POST /api/readings, replacement box onเลขมิเตอร์ที่กรอกไม่สอดคล้องกับการเปลี่ยนมิเตอร์ กรุณาตรวจสอบ
READING_FROZEN422POST /api/readings on an approved periodบิลอนุมัติแล้ว แก้ไขไม่ได้
RATE_NOT_FOUND422POST /api/bills/generateยังไม่ได้ตั้งค่าอัตราสำหรับรอบบิลนี้ กรุณาตั้งค่าที่หน้าตั้งค่า
ROOM_NOT_BILLABLE422Single-room generation on a Vacant or deactivated roomห้องนี้ไม่มีผู้เช่า จึงไม่ออกบิล
BILL_NOT_DRAFT409Recalculate — never approve, which counts a non-Draft id in already_approved (§3.3.9)บิลนี้ไม่ใช่ฉบับร่างแล้ว

PAYMENT_NOT_FOUND409The bill-scoped lookup of a live payment (Bill Detail resolving which payment to void)ไม่พบการชำระเงินที่ยังใช้งานอยู่สำหรับบิลนี้
PAYMENT_ALREADY_VOIDED409POST /payments/:id/void on a payment whose voided_at is already setการชำระเงินนี้ถูกยกเลิกไปแล้ว

4.15 Assumptions recorded by this section

  1. Arrears rows are the aged bills. Report 2's rows are Unpaid bills already past due, with the not-yet-due residual shown as a summary line above the table. This is the only way both halves of D-42 hold at once (§4.13.3).
  2. Rooms tiles exclude deactivated rooms. rooms_total, rooms_occupied and rooms_vacant all filter is_active = 1, matching D-47's rule that a deactivated room leaves every list except an explicit filter. Against the D-44 seed nothing is deactivated, so 80 / 67 / 13 is reproduced exactly.
  3. A missing rate aborts the whole run. D-17 does not say what happens when no rate row is eligible for a period. Refusing the run with RATE_NOT_FOUND before any write is safer than pricing a utility at zero, and it is the only branch that leaves nothing half-generated.
  4. Report 1's outstanding column reads the stored Unpaid status, so it includes bills that are not yet due. It is a billing summary, not an arrears list; report 2 is the arrears list. This mirrors D-41's outstanding_total and keeps billed − collected = outstanding true on every row.
  5. The single-bill approve reuses the bulk endpoint. Bill Detail posts an array of one to POST /api/bills/approve rather than adding a route of its own, so approval has exactly one code path and one set of guards and the JSON surface stays at the seven endpoints ADR §6.2 fixes.
  6. Receipt-number formatting is the one money-adjacent operation performed in JavaScript — String(seq).padStart(6, '0') is string work, not arithmetic, and does not breach D-16.
  7. Nothing here reintroduces a PRD §24 exclusion. No rent, no lease contract, no proration, no gateway, no slip, no QR, no automatic meter, no AI. Payment is cash at the counter; the only automation is the reminder job and the LINE messages.

5. LINE Official Account Integration & Notifications

This section specifies every part of the system that talks to LINE: the channel and its credentials, the inbound webhook, the one-time-code account linking flow, the seven Thai message templates, the send path with its retry and idempotency guarantees, the daily scheduler, and how all of it is tested without spending the free plan's monthly allowance. It is binding on routes/line.js, routes/internal.js, services/notify.js, services/linking.js, services/jobs.js and lib/templates.js.

Governing decisions

D-29 linking · D-30 delivery state and the notifications table · D-31 the exact Thai text · D-32 what runs the schedule · D-33 when reminders fire · D-34 which API and the quota · D-35 announcements · D-43 hosting and the fixed webhook origin · D-44 seed and placeholder LINE ids. ADR corrections ENG-3 (dedupe_key) and ENG-4 (link replies use the Reply API) apply here and are restated where they bite.

5.1 Scope and module map

ModuleOwnsNever does
routes/line.jsPOST /line/webhook: raw body, signature check, 200 before any work, hand the event array to services/linking.js.No SQL, no message text, no reply call.
services/linking.jsLink-code issue and match, line_followers upserts, tenant binding and unbinding, the three Reply API replies.Never pushes; never writes a notifications row (ENG-4).
services/notify.jsEnqueue, drain, retry, test-mode redirect, push and multicast, error mapping, the monthly quota count.Never opens the money transaction; never renders Thai text itself.
lib/templates.jsThe seven templates as pure functions of a variables object (D-31).No database access, no API call, no formatting logic of its own.
services/jobs.jsThe daily job body: select, enqueue, drain, summarise.Never changes a bill status — Overdue is derived (D-21).

The one architectural rule of this section: money state commits first, messages leave afterwards, outside the transaction (D-30). A LINE outage cannot roll back an approval or a payment, and no route waits on LINE inside a database transaction.

5.2 Official Account, channel and credentials

5.2.1 One-time console setup

  1. Create a LINE Business ID for the team (a shared account, not a student's personal ID — the credentials outlive the semester).
  2. In the LINE Official Account Manager, create the OA. Display name is the apartment name from Settings (demo seed: ศุภาลัย เรสซิเดนซ์) — never the software name (D-01).
  3. In the LINE Developers Console, create a provider and, under it, one Messaging API channel linked to that OA. One channel, one webhook URL, pointed at the deployed origin for the life of the project — see §5.8.4.
  4. Copy the Channel secret (Basic settings) and issue a long-lived Channel access token (Messaging API tab). Both go into environment variables and a password-protected team note, never into the repository (D-43).
  5. Set the webhook URL, enable Use webhook, press Verify, and set the response-mode and reply settings exactly as in the table below.
  6. Download the OA's QR code image and place it on the printable linking slip (§5.4.2).
Console settingRequired valueWhy
Webhook URLhttps://<render-host>/line/webhookOne fixed HTTPS origin for the life of the project (D-43).
Use webhookEnabledThe only path by which a userId ever reaches the system (D-29).
Response modeBotChat mode routes messages to a human operator and the webhook stops firing.
Auto-reply messagesDisabledThe app owns every reply, including the fixed auto-reply of D-29.
Greeting messageEnabled, text in §5.4.3The first thing a tenant sees after adding the OA must tell them to send the code.
Allow bot to join group chatsDisabledBills are per tenant. A group chat has no tenant.
Webhook redeliveryDisabledWe ACK before working, so a redelivery would only duplicate work the dedupe key then has to absorb.

5.2.2 Environment variables

VariableExample / shapeUsed byMissing at boot?
LINE_CHANNEL_SECRET32 hex charactersSignature verification on every webhook requestFatal — the process exits at startup
LINE_CHANNEL_ACCESS_TOKEN~170-character bearer tokenPush, multicast, replyFatal — the process exits at startup
JOB_TOKEN32+ random charactersX-Job-Token on POST /internal/jobs/daily (D-32)Fatal
LINE_MOCK1 or unsetInstalls the in-process fake client (§5.8.2); tests and CI onlyUnset = real client
TZAsia/BangkokEvery date the job and the templates compute (D-03)Fatal
Test mode is a database setting, not an environment variable

settings.line_test_mode (default 1) and settings.line_test_user_id live in the settings row (ADR §3.2, D-34). That is deliberate: the rehearsed demo turns real sending on and off from the Settings page in ten seconds, with no redeploy and no restart of a free instance that then takes 50 seconds to wake up.

5.3 The webhook endpoint

POST /line/webhook is one of the three routes that bypass the session middleware chain (ADR §2.1). It authenticates with X-Line-Signature and nothing else — no session, no CSRF token, no rate limiter.

ConditionResponseBodySide effect
Valid signature200emptyEvents processed after the response is written
Missing or bad signature401emptyNothing. One warn log line with the source IP, never the body
Body is not JSON but the signature is valid200emptyOne error log line; no events processed
Console Verify button (events: [], valid signature)200emptyNone — an empty array is a normal, expected request
A handler throws while processingalready 200—Logged; LINE is never told, because LINE disables an endpoint that keeps failing
 
// routes/line.js — mounted BEFORE express.json(), which would consume the raw bytes.
import { validateSignature } from '@line/bot-sdk';

router.post('/line/webhook',
  express.raw({ type: 'application/json', limit: '256kb' }),
  (req, res) => {
    const sig = req.get('x-line-signature') ?? '';
    if (!validateSignature(req.body, process.env.LINE_CHANNEL_SECRET, sig)) {
      log.warn({ evt: 'LINE_BAD_SIGNATURE', ip: req.ip });
      return res.sendStatus(401);                     // no envelope: LINE reads no body
    }
    res.sendStatus(200);                              // ACK FIRST — D-29, D-43
    let events = [];
    try { events = JSON.parse(req.body.toString('utf8')).events ?? []; }
    catch (e) { return log.error({ evt: 'LINE_BAD_JSON', msg: e.message }); }
    setImmediate(() => handleWebhookEvents(events)
      .catch((e) => log.error({ evt: 'LINE_EVENT_FAILED', msg: e.message })));
  });
Do not use the SDK's middleware() helper

@line/bot-sdk's middleware() runs the handlers and only then responds. On a Render free instance a cold start plus two queries can exceed LINE's timeout; repeated timeouts get the webhook disabled, and a disabled webhook silently kills the only channel by which a userId ever arrives (D-29, D-43). Verify the signature by hand, answer 200, work afterwards.

5.3.1 Local development — no tunnel, no second channel

ADR §2.2 fixes two environments, and local is the one where the webhook is disabled: the single Messaging API channel holds exactly one webhook URL and it belongs to the Render deployment (D-43). Local work therefore proceeds without a tunnel, and inbound events are exercised three ways:

  1. node --env-file=.env --watch server.js — the app listens on http://localhost:3000 with settings.line_test_mode = 1, so nothing outbound can reach a real tenant.
  2. Post a signed event to POST /line/webhook with the curl fixtures of §5.8.3 — a follow, a valid link code, and a tampered signature. This is the everyday loop and it exercises the real signature check, the real 200-first ACK and the real binding UPDATE.
  3. Run the integration tier with LINE_MOCK=1, which installs the in-process fake client of §5.8.2 and asserts on notifications rows rather than on an outbox.
  4. Once per milestone, do one real end-to-end link against the deployed instance: add the OA from a phone, send a code issued for a seeded tenant, and confirm LINK_OK names the room (§6.2.6).
A "(dev)" channel plus ngrok was considered and rejected

One channel holds exactly one webhook URL, so a tunnel would have to be re-pasted by whoever happened to be working on linking that day, and a free ngrok URL changes on every restart. A second channel would add a second secret, a second token and a second place for the webhook URL to be wrong — the same argument by which ADR §2.2 refuses a staging environment. The runbook rule that remains is one line: re-verify the webhook URL in the console after every deploy and before the demo — the procedure is runbook R2 (D-43).

5.4 Account linking

A tenant cannot read their own userId anywhere in the LINE app, so staff cannot type it and no import file can contain it. The only self-service path that uses nothing but the Messaging API is a printed one-time code (D-29). Uniqueness is on the tenant: tenants.id already gives it, so one LINE account may be bound to two Active tenancies — a parent renting two rooms — and every template names the room (ADR A-12).

5.4.1 The flow

sequenceDiagram
  autonumber
  participant A as "Admin, Tenants page"
  participant App as "Express app"
  participant DB as MySQL
  participant T as "Tenant on LINE"
  participant L as "LINE platform"
  A->>App: "POST /tenants/37/link-code"
  App->>DB: "INSERT link_codes: K7QM4P, expires in 7 days, single use"
  App-->>A: "printable slip: code plus OA QR"
  T->>L: "adds the Official Account as a friend"
  L->>App: "webhook, follow event"
  App-->>L: "200 immediately, then work"
  App->>DB: "upsert line_followers with userId and display name"
  T->>L: "sends K7QM4P as a chat message"
  L->>App: "webhook, message event"
  App-->>L: "200 immediately, then work"
  App->>DB: "SELECT link_codes: unused and not expired"
  alt code matched
    App->>DB: "UPDATE tenants line_user_id, expire code, audit TENANT_LINE_LINK"
    App->>L: "reply LINK_OK naming room 205"
  else no match or expired
    App->>L: "reply LINK_FAIL"
  end
  L->>T: "reply shown in the chat"
The webhook answers 200 before touching the database at either step. Both replies use the event's reply token, not a push (ENG-4), so neither consumes the 500-message monthly allowance.

5.4.2 Issuing a code

PropertyRuleExample
AlphabetABCDEFGHJKLMNPQRSTUVWXYZ23456789 — no O, 0, I, 1 (D-29)—
Length6 characters, drawn with crypto.randomInt, retried on the 1-in-10⁹ collision with UNIQUE(code)K7QM4P
Validity7 days from issue: expires_at = NOW() + INTERVAL 7 DAYissued 1 Sep 2026 → valid to 8 Sep 2026
UsesSingle use; used_at is stamped at the moment of binding—
Re-issuePressing สร้างรหัสผูก LINE again expires every outstanding code for that tenant and issues a new one — a tenant never holds two live codes—
WhereTenants page row action POST /tenants/:id/link-code, Admin only; the slip prints from GET /tenants/:id/link-code/slip—

The slip is A5 and carries four things: the room code, the tenant name, the code in 24 pt monospace, and the OA QR image. Its Thai instruction line is สแกน QR เพิ่มเพื่อน แล้วส่งรหัส {code} ในแชท.

5.4.3 Webhook event handling

Inbound text is normalised once before anything looks at it:

const candidate = raw.trim().toUpperCase().replace(/[\s-]/g, ''); // "k7qm 4p" and "k7qm-4p" both become "K7QM4P"
EventConditionActionReply (Reply API)
followalwaysINSERT line_followers … ON DUPLICATE KEY UPDATE display_name = VALUES(display_name), unfollowed_at = NULL; the profile name is fetched once with getProfilenone — the console's greeting message has already been delivered
unfollowalwaysUPDATE line_followers SET unfollowed_at = NOW(). The tenant binding is not cleared and nothing is surfaced in any badge or report (D-30)impossible — the user has left
message / text/^[A-Z2-9]{6}$/ matches and a live code existsBind, expire the code, write TENANT_LINE_LINK to audit_logLINK_OK
message / text6-character shape but no live codeNone. One info log line; the code is never echoed backLINK_FAIL
message / textanything elseNone — the OA is not a chatbot (D-29)AUTO_REPLY
message / sticker, image, otheralwaysNoneAUTO_REPLY
join, leave, postback, anything unknownalwaysIgnored, logged at debugnone

The binding itself is one conditional UPDATE, so two people racing the same code produce one winner and one LINK_FAIL:

-- inside one transaction, services/linking.js
UPDATE link_codes SET used_at = NOW()
 WHERE code = ? AND used_at IS NULL AND expires_at > NOW();      -- 0 rows => LINK_FAIL
UPDATE tenants
   SET line_user_id = ?, line_display_name = ?, line_linked_at = NOW()
 WHERE id = ? AND status = 'Active';

Console greeting message (configured in the OA Manager, not in code, and therefore the one outbound Thai string that does not live in lib/templates.js):

สวัสดีครับ/ค่ะ 🙏
กรุณาส่งรหัสผูก LINE 6 ตัวอักษรที่ได้รับจากสำนักงานนิติบุคคลในแชทนี้
เพื่อรับบิลค่าน้ำ ค่าไฟ และค่าส่วนกลางของห้องคุณผ่าน LINE

5.4.4 Unlink, relink, and tenants without LINE

SituationMechanismWhat the Admin sees
Admin unlinks (ยกเลิกการผูก)POST /tenants/:id/unlink-line clears the three line_* columns and audits TENANT_LINE_UNLINKThe Tenants row falls back to ยังไม่ได้ผูก LINE and a สร้างรหัสผูก LINE button
Tenant changes phone / LINE accountNo special case: issue a new code, the tenant sends it from the new account, the UPDATE overwrites line_user_idThe linked date refreshes; the old userId is not kept
Tenancy ends (D-08)Only that tenancy is unbound. A second Active tenancy sharing the same userId keeps receiving its own room's messagesNothing to do
Push returns an explicit 400 invalid userIdThe binding is cleared automatically — and never for ids beginning Useed (D-30, D-44)The row shows ส่งไม่สำเร็จ and reverts to unlinked
Tenant has no LINE at allNothing is sent. The notification row is written as skipped_no_line at enqueue time, never left queuedยังไม่ได้ส่ง — ไม่มี LINE on Monthly Billing and Bill Detail, plus the paper fallback พิมพ์ใบแจ้งหนี้ (D-28)

The seed makes this the visible common case, not an edge case: 67 Active tenants of whom exactly 54 carry a binding and 13 deliberately do not (D-44). A bulk approval of a whole period therefore renders 54 sent badges and 13 neutral ones, and the assertion after the run is that the linked count is still 54.

The 54 / 13 split needs a whole unapproved period

In the seed's current period 66 bills are already approved and only room 205 is Draft (§2.7, §6.2.5), so a bulk approve there approves one bill and writes one notifications row — room 205's tenant is linked, so it is 1 sent, 0 skipped. Any test that wants the 54 / 13 fan-out must first create a fully unapproved period: seed readings for the next period, POST /api/bills/generate, then approve all 67. The 54 / 13 figures also appear unconditionally on the announcement path, which fans out to all 67 tenants from a single click.

5.5 Message catalogue

Seven templates, all plain text, all built from braced placeholders only — no room number, month, amount, date, office hour or apartment name is ever hard-coded (D-31). Every template is one function in lib/templates.js; no message string appears at a call site.

renderTemplate(key, vars) → string // key ∈ BILL | BILL_CORRECTED | PRE_DUE | OVERDUE | PAID | ANNOUNCEMENT | LINK_OK | LINK_FAIL | AUTO_REPLY
Template keys are not notification types

notifications.type has exactly six values: BILL, BILL_CORRECTED, PRE_DUE, OVERDUE, PAID, ANNOUNCEMENT (ADR §3.3). LINK_OK, LINK_FAIL and AUTO_REPLY are reply-only template keys: they are sent with the Reply API, they write no notifications row, and they never appear in the monthly counter (ENG-4).

5.5.1 Trigger table

KeyTriggerAPInotifications rowCounts toward 500?
BILLอนุมัติและส่งบิล — single or bulk (D-19)Pushone per billyes
BILL_CORRECTEDRe-approval after a revert to Draft (D-22)Pushone per bill, own dedupe keyyes
PRE_DUEDaily job, due_date − today = 2 (D-33)Pushone per billyes
OVERDUEDaily job, days_overdue IN (1, 8, 15) (D-33)Pushone per bill per firing dayyes
PAIDCommit of ยืนยันรับเงินสด, after the receipt exists (D-24, D-27)Pushone per billyes
ANNOUNCEMENTส่งประกาศ, audience ทุกห้องที่มีผู้เช่า or เลือกชั้น (D-35)Multicast, ≤ 500 ids per requestone per recipient tenantyes, one per recipient
LINK_OK / LINK_FAIL / AUTO_REPLYInbound message event (D-29)Replynoneno

5.5.2 Placeholders

PlaceholderSourceFormatterPRD §28 value
{period_th}bills.period (2026-09)periodTh()กันยายน 2026
{room_code}bills.room_code_snapshot—205
{water_units} / {elec_units}bill_items.quantitynum(), trailing .00 dropped10 / 70
{water_rate} / {elec_rate} / {common_rate}bill_items.unit_price (frozen, D-17)moneyText()18 / 8 / 35
{water_amount} / {elec_amount} / {common_amount}bill_items.amountmoneyText()180 / 560 / 1,050
{area}bills.room_area_sqmnum()30
{total}bills.totalmoneyText()1,790
{due_date_th} / {paid_date_th}bills.due_date / payments.paid_atdateThLong()5 กันยายน 2026 / 3 กันยายน 2026
{days_overdue}DATEDIFF(CURDATE(), due_date)integer1
{receipt_no}receipts.receipt_no—REC-202609-000852
{office_hours} / {apartment_name}settings—จันทร์-ศุกร์ 09:00-17:00 น. / ศุภาลัย เรสซิเดนซ์
{previous_period_th}bill_items.previous_period, gap-covering bills only (D-14)periodTh()—
{title} / {body}announcements, ≤ 60 / ≤ 400 chars (D-35)——

Money in LINE is written 1,790 บาท — number, space, บาท, trailing .00 dropped, never the ฿ glyph, which renders inconsistently in the LINE client (D-03). Dates use the full Thai month and a Gregorian year (5 กันยายน 2026), never Buddhist Era. A missing placeholder is a thrown error, not an empty string: renderTemplate fails loudly rather than sending ห้อง undefined.

5.5.3 The templates, verbatim, each with the §28 worked example

(1) BILL — trigger: อนุมัติและส่งบิล.

🔔 ค่าใช้จ่ายประจำเดือน {period_th}
ห้อง {room_code}

💧 ค่าน้ำ {water_units} หน่วย × {water_rate} บาท = {water_amount} บาท

A gap-covering bill (D-14) appends one line: หมายเหตุ: ยอดนี้ครอบคลุมการใช้ตั้งแต่รอบ {previous_period_th}.

(2) BILL_CORRECTED — trigger: re-approval after ตีกลับเป็นฉบับร่าง (D-22). The BILL body, prefixed:

The literal (อีก 2 วัน) is safe precisely because the send day is fixed at due_date − 2; it is not a placeholder and must not be turned into one.

5.6 Sending

5.6.1 Push, multicast, and what is never used

APIUsed forBatchRows written
Push POST /v2/bot/message/pushEverything personalised: BILL, BILL_CORRECTED, PRE_DUE, OVERDUE, PAID, and every retry of a failed ANNOUNCEMENT rowone call per recipient — the text differs per roomone per recipient
Multicast POST /v2/bot/message/multicastANNOUNCEMENT only — identical text for everyonechunks of at most 500 distinct userIdsone per recipient regardless (D-30)
Reply POST /v2/bot/message/replyLINK_OK, LINK_FAIL, AUTO_REPLY, using the event's reply tokenone per inbound eventnone (ENG-4)
BroadcastNever. It reaches every OA follower, including ex-tenants and anyone who scanned the QR at the noticeboard (D-34).

With 67 rooms and 54 linked tenants, an announcement is one multicast request and 54 notification rows. The same announcement sent by push would be 54 requests for identical text.

5.6.2 Limits and the monthly allowance

LimitValueHead-room here
Free-plan messages per month500 push + multicast recipients (D-34)The binding constraint. See the worked budget below.
Recipients per multicast request500 userIds54 linked tenants = one request; the chunker exists for correctness, not for us
Message objects per request5We always send exactly 1
Text message length5,000 charactersThe longest template is under 300; announcements are capped at 60 + 400 by D-35
Request rate per channel2,000 requests/secondThe largest burst the system can produce is 20 pushes per drain call (D-19)
Reply messagesfree, do not countAll three link replies (ENG-4)

Worked monthly budget against the seed (54 linked tenants):

54 BILL + 54 PAID + 54 PRE_DUE = 162 + 10 late bills × 3 OVERDUE sends = 30 → 192 + 3 announcements × 54 recipients = 162 → 354 + a fourth announcement = 54 → 408 + a fifth announcement = 54 → 462 ⇒ Settings turns warn above 450

Settings shows one line, ส่งข้อความเดือนนี้: {N}/500, in warn colour above 450:

SELECT COUNT(*) AS n FROM notifications
 WHERE status = 'sent'
   AND send_date >= DATE_FORMAT(CURDATE(), '%Y-%m-01');

failed and skipped_no_line rows never inflate it (D-34). Nothing is throttled or blocked — exhausting the allowance silently stops delivery and is limitation (12) of D-05.

5.6.3 The notifications table in use

ColumnWritten whenRoom 205 BILL example
typeenqueueBILL
bill_id / announcement_idenqueue — exactly one is non-null118 / NULL
tenant_idenqueue37
recipient_user_idenqueue — snapshot of tenants.line_user_id, the intended recipient even in test modeU4af4980…, or NULL when unlinked
statusenqueue, then every attemptqueued → sent
attempt_count+1 on every attempt; automatic retries stop at 31
retry_keyenqueue (UUID()), reused by automatic retries, regenerated by the manual button3f2b…
http_status / last_erroron failure only; last_error is the LINE message truncated to 255 charsNULL
last_attempt_atevery attempt2026-09-01 10:12:04
send_dateenqueue, always CURDATE()2026-09-01
dedupe_keygenerated, UNIQUE (ENG-3)BILL:118:0:37:2026-09-01

Enqueue is an insert-if-absent that never disturbs an existing row, so approving twice produces no second message:

INSERT INTO notifications
  (type, bill_id, announcement_id, tenant_id, recipient_user_id, status, retry_key, send_date)
SELECT 'BILL', b.id, NULL, b.tenant_id, t.line_user_id,
       IF(t.line_user_id IS NULL, 'skipped_no_line', 'queued'), UUID(), CURDATE()
  FROM bills b JOIN tenants t ON t.id = b.tenant_id
 WHERE b.id = ?
ON DUPLICATE KEY UPDATE id = LAST_INSERT_ID(id);
-- affectedRows: 1 = newly enqueued, 0 = already enqueued today (the row is set to its
-- current values, which MySQL reports as 0 changed rows — it is never 2 here).

The branch is therefore written if (result.affectedRows !== 1) { /* already enqueued today, send nothing */ }. LAST_INSERT_ID(id) still hands back the existing row's id, so the caller can read the row either way.

An unlinked tenant is written straight to skipped_no_line at enqueue time, never left queued for a drain that would only discover the same thing.

5.6.4 Drain, retry and idempotency

Five layers, all in the database, none in process memory (ADR §7.6):

#LayerCloses
1UNIQUE(dedupe_key) on CONCAT_WS(':', type, IFNULL(bill_id,0), IFNULL(announcement_id,0), tenant_id, send_date)Two rows for the same message type, bill and tenant on one day — whatever calls the code. D-30's literal UNIQUE(type, bill_id, announcement_id, tenant_id, send_date) cannot do this: InnoDB treats NULLs as distinct and one of the two id columns is always NULL (ENG-3)
2attempt_countAn endless automatic retry loop. Automatic retries stop at 3; the Admin's manual button is never capped (D-30, D-33)
3X-Line-Retry-Key header carrying notifications.retry_keyA push that succeeded at LINE but whose result was lost before it could be recorded — LINE discards the repeat
4SELECT GET_LOCK('notif_drain', 0), released in a finallyThe approve result screen and the daily job pushing the same rows concurrently. Fallback if the provider blocks it: FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED inside the drain transaction
5UNIQUE(job_name, run_date) on job_runsA retrying scheduler running the day twice (D-32)

The drain selection, run under the lock, at most 20 rows per call (D-19):

SELECT id, type, bill_id, announcement_id, tenant_id, recipient_user_id, retry_key, attempt_count
  FROM notifications
 WHERE (status = 'queued' OR (status = 'failed' AND attempt_count < 3))
   AND send_date >= CURDATE() - INTERVAL 7 DAY
 ORDER BY id
 LIMIT 20;

The seven-day window is deliberate: a BILL that has failed every retry for a fortnight must not surprise a tenant who already paid at the counter. Anything older is handed over on paper (D-28) or re-sent by hand.

Retry key reuse — this specification narrows ADR §7.6 layer 3

ADR §7.6 says the retry key is "reused on every attempt for that row". This specification narrows that to automatic attempts only: automatic retries reuse retry_key, so a push that actually landed is not duplicated, while the Admin's manual ส่งแจ้งเตือนอีกครั้ง regenerates it before sending.

The reason is that the manual button is the tool of last resort on the day an automatic send failed, and D-30 / D-33 say it is never capped. A reused key makes LINE deduplicate that resend, so the uncapped button would silently do nothing — the one failure mode it exists to fix. The narrowing is recorded here as an override because the ADR outranks this document (see the Introduction's precedence rule); everything else in §7.6 layer 3 stands unchanged.

5.6.5 Send path and error mapping

flowchart TD
  A["drain row: queued, or failed with attempt_count below 3"]
  B{"recipient_user_id present"}
  C["status skipped_no_line, no API call"]
  D{"settings.line_test_mode"}
  N{"settings.line_test_user_id present"}
  P["swallow: no API call, status sent, last_error records the swallow"]
  E["target = settings.line_test_user_id"]
  F["target = recipient_user_id"]
  G["push, header X-Line-Retry-Key = retry_key"]
  H{"HTTP result"}
  I["status sent, attempt_count plus one"]
  J["status failed, store http_status and last_error"]
  K{"400 and message names an invalid userId"}
  L["clear the tenant binding, unless the id starts with Useed"]
  M["leave the binding, retry tomorrow"]
  A --> B
  B -- no --> C
  B -- yes --> D
  D -- "on, the default" --> N
  D -- off --> F
  N -- "no, the seed default" --> P
  N -- yes --> E
  E --> G
  F --> G
  G --> H
  H -- 200 --> I
  H -- "4xx, 5xx or timeout" --> J
  J --> K
  K -- yes --> L
  K -- no --> M
Test mode rewrites only the target; recipient_user_id on the row still records who the message was for, so delivery counts stay honest during a rehearsal (D-34).
Test mode with no whitelisted recipient — the swallow branch

This is the one rule stated here and cited everywhere else (§2.3.2, §5.8.1, §6.5.1). When line_test_mode = 1 and line_test_user_id IS NULL — which is what seed.sql ships (§2.7) and therefore the state a fresh clone runs in — the sender makes no API call at all and writes the row as status = 'sent', attempt_count = 1, http_status = NULL, last_error = 'test mode, no whitelisted recipient'. It is sent rather than a fifth status because every screen, counter and test then reads the demo state exactly as it will read the real one; last_error is the audit trail that says nothing left the building. It does not count toward the monthly 500, because nothing was pushed.

Pushing to a NULL target is never attempted: LINE answers 400, the row lands failed, and the rehearsal assertions of §5.8.1 and §6.5.1 — which both require status='sent' — would fail on an untouched checkout. Setting line_test_user_id to a real developer userId in Settings switches the same path back to a genuine push.

ResultStatus writtenAutomatic retry?Extra action
Test mode on, line_test_user_id NULL — no call madesent—last_error = 'test mode, no whitelisted recipient'; does not count toward the monthly 500 (D-34, and the callout above)
200sent—Counts toward the monthly 500
400, message names an invalid to / userIdfailedNo — attempt_count is set straight to 3Clear the tenant's binding (D-30); skip ids beginning Useed (D-44)
400, any other messagefailedNo — a malformed request will not fix itselfLogged at error with the request body shape, never the token
401 / 403failedNo — attempt_count set to 3Token is wrong or revoked; runbook R3, not a retry — and the backlog needs re-arming by hand, because attempt_count = 3 puts these rows outside the drain's retry window
429failedYes, by tomorrow's jobIn practice this means the monthly allowance is gone
5xx, socket timeout, DNS failurefailedYes, up to attempt_count 3Same retry_key reused
200 but the tenant blocked the OAsent—Indistinguishable from delivery. LINE reports both as success; the word "blocked" appears in no badge, report or test case (D-30, D-05 item 9)

5.6.6 Announcements

Announcements do not go through the row-by-row drain, because they exist to be one request. sendAnnouncement() runs after the announcement row is committed:

  1. Resolve the audience: all = every room with an Active tenant; floors = the same, filtered by rooms.floor IN (…) (D-35).
  2. Insert one notifications row per Active tenant in the audience — queued when linked, skipped_no_line when not.
  3. Multicast the rendered text to the distinct linked userIds in chunks of at most 500 (one chunk, always, at this building's size).
  4. Bulk-update that chunk's rows to sent, or to failed with the chunk's http_status.
  5. Return ส่งประกาศถึง {rooms} ห้อง · ส่งสำเร็จ {x} · ไม่มี LINE {y} · ส่งไม่สำเร็จ {z}.

A failed chunk is retried by the daily job as individual pushes — at most 54 messages, one per row, using the ordinary drain path. That is one code path fewer than a chunk-level retry and the quota cost is identical.

5.6.7 Delivery status shown to admins

Row statusBadgeThai labelWhere
sentส่งแล้วส่งแล้วMonthly Billing column, Bill Detail, Announcements list
queued—no badge; the row renders —Same three surfaces. The in-flight state is carried by the approve result screen's progress bar, labelled กำลังส่ง {x}/{N} — a batch counter, not a per-bill badge
skipped_no_lineยังไม่ได้ส่ง — ไม่มี LINEยังไม่ได้ส่ง — ไม่มี LINESame three surfaces
failedส่งไม่สำเร็จส่งไม่สำเร็จ, last_error on hoverSame three surfaces, plus the approve result summary
ส่งแล้ว means recorded, not delivered

The badge is rendered from notifications.status = 'sent', and §5.6.5's swallow branch writes that status without making an API call at all whenever line_test_mode = 1 and line_test_user_id IS NULL — which is precisely what seed.sql ships. On an untouched clone every approval therefore paints ส่งแล้ว while nothing has left the building. The row's last_error carries 'test mode, no whitelisted recipient', and that field is the only thing on any screen that distinguishes the two cases; Bill Detail's delivery panel shows it verbatim.

Two consequences that matter more than the wording. (1) Before a demo, delivery has to be arranged deliberately, not assumed from a green badge — the three-step เตรียมสาธิต LINE procedure in §5.8.4. (2) A ส่งแล้ว produced by the swallow branch does not count toward the monthly 500 (§5.6.2), because nothing was pushed.

D-30's three badges are exhaustive and this specification adds none. A row is in flight only between the approve commit and the last drain call, and the operator watching that window is looking at the approve result screen, whose .progress bar already reads กำลังส่ง {x}/{N} for the whole batch. Introducing a fourth per-bill badge would amend a product decision from an engineering document, and would leave the UI design and the test plan rendering a queued row two other ways. Bill Detail additionally shows a small delivery panel — type, attempt count, last attempt time, the error text if any — and the Admin-only button ส่งแจ้งเตือนอีกครั้ง (POST /bills/:id/notify, POST-Redirect-GET, never capped).

Approval is never blocked by delivery (D-30). The approve result screen reports, verbatim: อนุมัติ {N} ใบ · ส่ง LINE สำเร็จ {x} · ยังไม่ได้ส่ง (ไม่มี LINE) {y} · ส่งไม่สำเร็จ {z}, listing and linking the failed and unsent rooms so the office can print those bills instead (D-19, D-28).

5.6.8 Notification row lifecycle

stateDiagram-v2
  [*] --> queued: "enqueue, tenant is linked"
  [*] --> skipped_no_line: "enqueue, tenant has no line_user_id"
  queued --> sent: "push or multicast returned 200"
  queued --> failed: "error or timeout, attempt_count plus one"
  failed --> queued: "manual ส่งแจ้งเตือนอีกครั้ง, new retry_key"
  failed --> sent: "automatic retry succeeded"
  failed --> failed: "automatic retry failed, stops at attempt_count 3"
  sent --> [*]
  skipped_no_line --> [*]
  note right of skipped_no_line
    Never becomes queued on its own.
    Linking the tenant later does not
    resend an old bill, it only affects
    the next enqueue.
  end note
One row per recipient for its whole life. Every attempt rewrites this row; nothing appends (D-30).

5.7 Scheduler

5.7.1 Two schedules, deliberately separate

ScheduleCronTargetAuthPurpose
Daily notification job0 9 * * * (timezone Asia/Bangkok in the scheduler UI)POST /internal/jobs/dailyX-Job-TokenPRE_DUE, OVERDUE, drain, retries (D-32, D-33)
Keep-alive ping*/10 * * * *GET /healthzpublicA Render free instance sleeps after ~15 minutes; a once-a-day request keeps it awake for one minute, not one day (D-43)

There is no node-cron and no setInterval anywhere in the process (D-32): an in-process timer never fires on a sleeping instance, which would make the headline automatic-notification feature undemonstrable. If cron-job.org's free tier is unavailable, the documented substitute is a Render Cron Job with the same cron expression and the same header.

Timezone handling, in three places and no others

(1) The scheduler is configured with the Asia/Bangkok timezone, so 0 9 * * * is 09:00 local — never UTC. (2) The process runs with TZ=Asia/Bangkok and every pooled connection runs SET time_zone = '+07:00', so CURDATE() inside the job is the Bangkok date. (3) due_date and send_date are DATE columns read with dateStrings: true, so no JS Date constructor can shift them across a day boundary. Together these are why no date arithmetic happens in JavaScript at all (ADR §6.5, A-5).

5.7.2 The job

flowchart TD
  C["cron-job.org, 09:00 Asia/Bangkok"] --> J["POST /internal/jobs/daily"]
  J --> T{"X-Job-Token valid"}
  T -- no --> R["401, empty body, nothing runs"]
  T -- yes --> G{"INSERT job_runs, unique on job_name and run_date"}
  G -- "duplicate key" --> NOOP["200 with already_ran true, no messages"]
  G -- inserted --> S1["enqueue PRE_DUE where due_date minus today equals 2"]
  S1 --> S2["enqueue OVERDUE where days_overdue in 1, 8, 15"]
  S2 --> S3["GET_LOCK notif_drain, drain queued and failed below three attempts"]
  S3 --> S4["write summary_json and finished_at, return counts"]
  MAN["Settings button รันงานแจ้งเตือนตอนนี้"] --> S1
The manual button runs the identical code with job_name='daily_manual' and never consults the job_runs guard, so a demo at 14:00 still works after the 09:00 cron has run (D-32). Safety then rests entirely on the dedupe key, which is enough.

The two selections, verbatim in intent (D-33). Both exclude Paid bills and bills whose most recent payment was voided (D-25):

-- PRE_DUE: exactly two days before the due date
SELECT b.id, b.tenant_id FROM bills b
 WHERE b.status = 'Unpaid'
   AND DATEDIFF(b.due_date, CURDATE()) = 2
   AND NOT EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM payments p
                    WHERE p.bill_id = b.id AND p.voided_at IS NOT NULL
                      AND p.paid_at = (SELECT MAX(p2.paid_at) FROM payments p2 WHERE p2.bill_id = b.id));

-- OVERDUE: day 1, day 8, day 15 past due — and never again
SELECT b.id, b.tenant_id FROM bills b
 WHERE b.status = 'Unpaid'
   AND DATEDIFF(CURDATE(), b.due_date) IN (1, 8, 15)
   AND NOT EXISTS ( … same voided-payment clause … );

At most four automatic messages per bill in its entire life. Nothing after day 15, nothing to a Paid bill, nothing outside 09:00, no evening or weekend variant. Worked example, room 205, due 5 Sep 2026:

Date, 09:00ConditionMessageIf paid on 3 Sep 2026
1 Sep 2026approval — not the jobBILLsent
3 Sep 2026DATEDIFF(due, today) = 2PRE_DUEsent — the bill is still Unpaid at 09:00; the tenant pays at the counter later that day
3 Sep 2026, at the counterpayment commitsPAID with REC-202609-000852sent
6 Sep 2026days_overdue = 1OVERDUEnot sent — status is Paid
13 Sep 2026days_overdue = 8OVERDUEnot sent
20 Sep 2026days_overdue = 15OVERDUEnot sent
21 Sep 2026 onward—nothing, ever—

Response shape (the standard envelope, ADR §6.3):

{ "data": { "job": "daily", "run_date": "2026-09-03", "already_ran": false,
            "pre_due": 12, "overdue": 5, "retried": 1,
            "sent": 17, "failed": 1, "skipped_no_line": 3 } }

The Settings button รันงานแจ้งเตือนตอนนี้ posts to POST /settings/run-daily-job (Admin only, CSRF-protected, POST-Redirect-GET) and reports the same numbers in Thai: ส่งแจ้งเตือนก่อนครบกำหนด {a} ราย · เลยกำหนด {b} ราย · ส่งซ้ำที่ล้มเหลว {c} ราย (D-32). The job also drains anything a bulk approval left queued (D-19) — it is the delivery safety net, which is why A-14 puts bulk approve above it in the cut order.

5.7.3 Running it locally and in deployment

# local — identical code path, no scheduler needed
curl -sS -X POST http://localhost:3000/internal/jobs/daily \
     -H "X-Job-Token: $JOB_TOKEN" | jq

# production smoke test after a deploy
curl -sS -X POST https://<render-host>/internal/jobs/daily \
     -H "X-Job-Token: $JOB_TOKEN" -i     # expect 200 and already_ran true after 09:00

Idempotency of the daily path is testable in one line: call it twice and assert the second response carries "already_ran": true and that SELECT COUNT(*) FROM notifications WHERE send_date = CURDATE() is unchanged. Then call the manual path and assert it runs but still sends nothing twice — the dedupe key absorbs it.

5.8 Testing LINE

5.8.1 What is asserted, and where

TierTestAssertion
UnitrenderTemplate('BILL', §28 fixture)Byte-for-byte equal to the rendered example in §5.5.3. This test must never go red — it is the same fixture as the 10 × 18 / 70 × 8 / 30 × 35 = 1,790 arithmetic test (ADR §6.9)
UnitrenderTemplate with a missing placeholderThrows; never emits undefined
UnitSignature verification over a fixture bodyCorrect signature passes; one flipped byte fails
UnitCode normalisation"k7qm 4p", " K7QM-4P " and "K7QM4P" all normalise equal
IntegrationApprove the same bill twiceOne notifications row, one push; the second approve returns 200 with approved: 0, already_approved: 1 and queues nothing — never a 409 (§3.3.9, §4.8.1)
IntegrationDaily job twice on one day, then the manual buttonSecond call already_ran: true; manual call runs and still sends nothing twice
IntegrationBulk approve a whole unapproved period — seed readings for the next period, POST /api/bills/generate, then approve all 67 (the seed's current period has only room 205 left Draft, so it cannot produce this split — §5.4.4)54 rows sent, 13 rows skipped_no_line, and the linked-tenant count is still 54 (D-44)
IntegrationBulk approve the seed's current period as shipped1 approved, 1 row sent, 0 skipped_no_line
IntegrationPush returns 400 invalid userId for a Useed… idRow failed, binding not cleared
IntegrationSigned webhook with a valid code, then the same code againFirst binds and replies LINK_OK; second replies LINK_FAIL
RehearsalThe full demo path with test mode ON and line_test_user_id NULL as seededBoth the BILL and the PAID row recorded as status='sent' through the swallow branch of §5.6.5 — no API call is made; arithmetic asserted, calendar never asserted

5.8.2 Mocking the Messaging API

There is no HTTP mock server and no recorded-cassette library. services/notify.js holds one module-level client and exports a setter:

// services/notify.js
let client = process.env.LINE_MOCK === '1' ? fakeClient() : realClient();
export const setLineClient = (c) => { client = c; };   // unit tests only

// test/helpers/fakeLine.js — 20 lines, the whole mock
export const fakeClient = () => {
  const outbox = [];
  return {
    outbox,
    pushMessage: async (to, msg, key) => { outbox.push({ api: 'push', to, msg, key }); return { status: 200 }; },
    multicast:   async (to, msg, key) => { outbox.push({ api: 'multicast', to, msg, key }); return { status: 200 }; },
    replyMessage: async (token, msg)  => { outbox.push({ api: 'reply', token, msg }); return { status: 200 }; },
  };
};

Integration tests run the real server with LINE_MOCK=1 and assert on the notifications rows, not on the outbox — the rows are what the Admin screens, the monthly counter and the reports read, so asserting on them tests the thing that ships. A failure is simulated by handing the unit test a client whose pushMessage rejects with a given status. LINE_MOCK=1 is set in CI and in .env; it is never set on Render.

5.8.3 Simulating webhook events with curl

The signature is base64(HMAC-SHA256(rawBody, channelSecret)). Signing the exact bytes matters: reformatting the JSON changes the signature.

# 1) follow event — registers the userId in line_followers
BODY='{"destination":"Uoa0000000000000000000000000000","events":[{"type":"follow","mode":"active","timestamp":1757203200000,"source":{"type":"user","userId":"Udev0000000000000000000000000001"},"webhookEventId":"01J9FOLLOW","deliveryContext":{"isRedelivery":false},"replyToken":"00000000000000000000000000000000"}]}'
SIG=$(printf '%s' "$BODY" | openssl dgst -sha256 -hmac "$LINE_CHANNEL_SECRET" -binary | base64)
curl -sS -i -X POST http://localhost:3000/line/webhook \
  -H "Content-Type: application/json" -H "X-Line-Signature: $SIG" --data-binary "$BODY"
# expect: HTTP/1.1 200 OK, and one row in line_followers

# 2) message event carrying the link code
BODY='{"destination":"Uoa0000000000000000000000000000","events":[{"type":"message","mode":"active","timestamp":1757203260000,"source":{"type":"user","userId":"Udev0000000000000000000000000001"},"webhookEventId":"01J9MSG","deliveryContext":{"isRedelivery":false},"replyToken":"00000000000000000000000000000001","message":{"id":"1","type":"text","quoteToken":"q1","text":"K7QM4P"}}]}'
SIG=$(printf '%s' "$BODY" | openssl dgst -sha256 -hmac "$LINE_CHANNEL_SECRET" -binary | base64)
curl -sS -i -X POST http://localhost:3000/line/webhook \
  -H "Content-Type: application/json" -H "X-Line-Signature: $SIG" --data-binary "$BODY"
# expect: 200, tenants.line_user_id set, link_codes.used_at stamped, audit row TENANT_LINE_LINK

# 3) tampered signature
curl -sS -i -X POST http://localhost:3000/line/webhook \
  -H "Content-Type: application/json" -H "X-Line-Signature: bm90LWEtc2lnbmF0dXJl" --data-binary "$BODY"
# expect: 401, and nothing written

With LINE_MOCK=1 the reply token above is never sent anywhere, so the fabricated value is harmless. Against the real dev channel a reply token is single-use and expires in about a minute, so replies to a hand-made token fail with 400 — the binding still happens, which is the part under test.

5.8.4 The one channel

AspectRuleWhy it is not negotiable
Webhook URLhttps://<render-host>/line/webhook, fixed for the project's lifeA channel holds exactly one URL; the deployment is the only stable origin (D-43)
CredentialsRender environment variables and a password-protected team note. The same secret and token are copied into each student's local .env, where the webhook is never calledRotating a leaked token means re-verifying one URL, not four
FriendsThe team's own accounts, plus the at most three real accounts used in the graded runEvery follower costs quota on an announcement
QuotaOne allowance of 500 push + multicast recipients per month for the whole projectThere is no second pool to fall back on — see the budget in §5.6.2
line_test_modeON by default in every environment, switched off only for the rehearsed demo (D-34)It is a Settings row, so the switch takes ten seconds and no redeploy
Two rules that protect the graded demo

(1) Test mode defaults ON in every environment, so a stray bulk approve cannot spend the month's allowance. (2) The seed's bindings are placeholder ids of the form Useed0000…{tenant_id}, which are never valid push targets and which the auto-unbind explicitly skips — so a real 400 against seeded data cannot wipe the 54 bindings the demo depends on (D-34, D-44).

5.8.5 เตรียมสาธิต LINE — the demo-day preparation procedure

Nothing is delivered from an untouched seed, in either mode

Room 205's tenant carries Useed0000000000000000000000000037, which §5.4.4 and D-44 state is never a valid push target, and settings.line_test_user_id ships NULL, which §5.6.5 makes the swallow branch. So on a fresh clone the two available states are both wrong for a demo: with test mode ON no API call is made and the badge still turns ส่งแล้ว (§5.6.7); with test mode OFF the push is attempted against the Useed… id, LINE answers 400, and the row lands ส่งไม่สำเร็จ. Neither shows an examiner a message on a phone. The three steps below are what makes the LINE half of the demo real, and they are done before the examiner is in the room.

  1. Give the demo a real recipient. Two ways, and the choice is between them, not both:
    • Bind a real account (preferred). On ผู้เช่า, press สร้างรหัสผูก LINE for room 205's tenant, then send that 6-character code from the team's own LINE account to the Official Account. The webhook overwrites tenants.line_user_id, replacing the Useed… placeholder with a genuine userId and auditing TENANT_LINE_LINK (§5.4.3). The demo's BILL and PAID messages then arrive on that phone. Leave โหมดทดสอบ LINE ON: with a whitelisted id set it still redirects, and with it unset the swallow branch would eat the message — so if you take this route, also set line_test_user_id as below, or switch test mode off deliberately and accept that all 67 rooms would push for real on a bulk approve.
    • Whitelist a team account and leave test mode ON (safest). On ตั้งค่า, set บัญชีรับข้อความทดสอบ (settings.line_test_user_id) to the team's own userId and keep โหมดทดสอบ LINE ON. Every outbound message is redirected to that one account while notifications.recipient_user_id still records the intended tenant (D-34), so the delivery badges and the quota counter stay honest and a slip cannot spend the allowance on 67 rooms.
  2. Confirm the mode on ตั้งค่า, by reading it. The Settings page states both values in one line — โหมดทดสอบ LINE: เปิด · บัญชีรับข้อความทดสอบ: ตั้งค่าแล้ว — and the quota line ส่งข้อความเดือนนี้: {N}/500. Never trust the default: the whole point of §5.6.5's swallow branch is that a wrong configuration is invisible in the badges.
  3. Send one test bill before the examiner arrives. Run npm run db:demo-arm (§2.7), then walk room 205 through จดมิเตอร์ → ส่งให้นิติตรวจสอบ → อนุมัติและส่งบิล once and watch the message land on the phone. Then run npm run db:demo-arm again to re-arm the room for the real demo. A rehearsal that stops at the badge has verified nothing that §5.6.7 does not already warn about.

The whole procedure costs about five minutes and is the one part of the demo that cannot be recovered in the room. It is also the reason the LINE half of the demo is scripted as one bill to one account and never as a 67-room bulk approve: at three real followers the free plan's 500 messages are inexhaustible, and at 67 they are not (§5.6.2, R-02).

5.9 Limitations and recorded assumptions

Carried verbatim from D-05, the items this section owns:

  • (7) No tenant-facing web login — tenants only ever receive LINE messages.
  • (8) No email and no SMS channel.
  • (9) The system cannot tell a delivered LINE message from one silently dropped because the tenant blocked the Official Account — LINE reports both as success.
  • (12) The free plan's 500-message monthly allowance is displayed but never enforced; exhausting it silently stops delivery.

Nothing here reintroduces a PRD §24 exclusion: no mobile app (the tenant's client is LINE itself), no payment gateway, no QR payment, no slip upload, no IoT meter, no AI. The LINE messages are notifications; every payment still happens in cash at the counter.

Assumptions recorded by this section
  1. Flex Messages are forbidden in v1 by D-31 — "seven plain-text templates, no Flex Messages, no images, no rich menu". The brief for this section asked for Flex payloads for the bill and the receipt confirmation; no payload is carried anywhere in this document, because a copy-pasteable one is an invitation to build it (R-08). If a later term adopts Flex, lib/templates.js gains a second renderer and no other module changes.
  2. The unfollow event exists even though D-30 says blocking is invisible. LINE does deliver an unfollow event, and this section records it on line_followers for the record. It changes no tenant binding and surfaces in no badge, count or report, so D-30's user-visible rule holds exactly as written — and blocking after an unfollow is still undetectable at send time, which is why limitation (9) stands.
  3. No fourth delivery badge. An earlier draft of this specification introduced กำลังส่ง for status='queued'. It is withdrawn: D-30's three badges are exhaustive, and the in-flight window a 67-bill batch genuinely has (up to three minutes, D-45 target 4) is already shown by the approve result screen's กำลังส่ง {x}/{N} progress bar. A queued row renders — (§5.6.7).
  4. The seven-day retry window on the drain selection is an engineering choice, not a decision-log rule: it stops a fortnight-old failed BILL surprising a tenant who already paid.
  5. The manual resend regenerates retry_key while automatic retries reuse it (§5.6.4). This narrows ADR §7.6 layer 3, which says the key is reused on every attempt; without the narrowing the manual button would be silently deduplicated by LINE, contradicting D-30 and D-33's "never capped". It is recorded as a decision callout in §5.6.4, not as a silent edit.
  6. Published LINE platform limits (2,000 requests/second, 500 recipients per multicast, 5,000-character text, 5 message objects per request) are quoted from the Messaging API documentation as of kickoff and are re-verified in the console before the demo. Only the 500-message monthly allowance is load-bearing, and it comes from D-34.
  7. One Messaging API channel and no local webhook. ADR §2.2 defines exactly two environments and local is the one with the webhook disabled, so the “(dev) channel plus a tunnel” option is rejected in §5.3.1: inbound events are exercised by signed curl fixtures and by one real link per milestone against the deployed instance.
  8. X-Line-Retry-Key is settable on the pinned SDK major (ADR A-13.4). If it is not, idempotency layers 1, 2, 4 and 5 still stand and the only exposure is a duplicate message after a lost response.

6. Project Structure, Environment & Implementation Plan

This section turns ADR-001 into something a student can check out on a Monday morning and run. It fixes the folder tree, the environment variables, the npm scripts, the deploy procedure, the milestone plan for a 3–4 person team over one 14-week semester, the quality strategy and the risk register. Every name below either comes from ADR-001 §5–§6 verbatim or is listed as a new identifier in the callout at the end of this section.

Three structural facts that shape everything in this section
  • One repository, one deployable process, zero build steps (ADR A-1, D-43). The "frontend" is views/ + public/ inside the same repo. There is no second package.json, no bundler, no second deploy target, and therefore no npm run build.
  • No migration framework (ADR A-3). The database is rebuilt from a committed db/schema.sql + db/seed.sql. The script called db:migrate below applies the schema to an empty database; it does not step through versioned files.
  • The graded deliverable is the PRD §28 demo path. The milestone order below is chosen so that room 205 produces 1,790.00 THB end-to-end as early as week 8, and with LINE by week 9 — five weeks before the defence.

6.1 Repository layout

Repository name juristic-platform, default branch main, Node.js 24.x, ESM everywhere ("type": "module"). Ten runtime dependencies, three dev dependencies, package-lock.json committed.

6.1.1 Backend and repository root

juristic-platform/
├─ server.js                    # app wiring, middleware order, graceful shutdown

├─ package-lock.json            # committed; npm ci is the only reproducible install

The layering rule, in one sentence

Routes do HTTP, services do transactions and rules and their own SQL, lib/ is pure functions. A req object never reaches a service; a SQL string never appears in a route; an arithmetic operator never appears next to money in JavaScript. These three greps are the entire code review for structure: grep -rn "req\." services/, grep -rniE "select|insert|update" routes/, grep -rn "\*\|+" lib/money.js must each return nothing.

The Content-Security-Policy, written out

helmet is the first middleware in server.js and this object is the only place a CSP is written. It is not optional and it is not a hardening pass for later: helmet's default policy is default-src 'self', which blocks the Chart.js tag on the first day of M0, so the two remote origins this application actually uses have to be named before the Dashboard can ever draw a chart.

// server.js — before express.static, before the session (ADR §2.1 middleware order)
import helmet from 'helmet';

app.use(helmet({
  contentSecurityPolicy: {
    useDefaults: false,
    directives: {
      'default-src':     ["'self'"],
      'script-src':      ["'self'", 'https://cdnjs.cloudflare.com'],   // Chart.js only
      'style-src':       ["'self'", 'https://fonts.googleapis.com'],   // the Noto Sans Thai stylesheet
      'font-src':        ["'self'", 'https://fonts.gstatic.com', 'data:'],
      'img-src':         ["'self'", 'data:'],                          // the OA QR on the link slip
      'connect-src':     ["'self'"],                                   // fetch to our own /api/* only
      'object-src':      ["'none'"],
      'base-uri':        ["'self'"],
      'form-action':     ["'self'"],
      'frame-ancestors': ["'none'"],
    },
  },
}));
Inline strategy: no inline scripts at all — no nonce, no 'unsafe-inline'

The chosen strategy is the absence of inline script, not a per-request nonce. A nonce means generating a random value per request, putting it on res.locals, threading it through views/layout.ejs and remembering it on every <script> any page ever adds — middleware plus a discipline that is forgotten the first time someone is debugging at 2am, and forgotten silently, because a missing nonce looks exactly like a broken feature. The six files of public/js/ in §6.1.2 are already external files, so script-src 'self' https://cdnjs.cloudflare.com costs this project nothing and there is nothing left to forget. The rule that keeps it true: an EJS page never contains <script>…code…</script>. Server values reach a script through a data- attribute on an element (<div id="chart" data-period="2026-09">), which is how public/js/charts.js already learns its period.

Inline style="…" attributes are refused for the same reason. Anything whose geometry is computed — the approve result screen's กำลังส่ง {x}/{N} progress bar, the reading round's X/Y counter — sets it from JavaScript (el.style.width = …), which is a CSSOM write and is not governed by style-src. No stylesheet is ever built by string concatenation.

The Thai font is loaded from Google Fonts, not self-hosted

Decision: one <link rel="stylesheet" href="https://fonts.googleapis.com/css2?family=Noto+Sans+Thai:wght@400;500;600&display=swap"> in views/layout.ejs, which is what style-src and font-src above exist for. Self-hosting under public/fonts/ would drop both remote origins from the policy and survive an offline demo, at the cost of four woff2 files (~600 KB) in a repository that otherwise has no binary assets, plus a @font-face block to maintain. The trade-off, stated once: with no internet the Google stylesheet does not load and the UI falls back to the next family in the stack — font-family: "Noto Sans Thai", "Sarabun", "Leelawadee UI", "Thonburi", sans-serif — so Thai still renders in a system face and only the letterforms change; nothing breaks, nothing shifts by more than a line. The same outage takes Chart.js with it, which is the real loss, and it is why the demo-day fallback assumes an offline demo shows no Dashboard chart and says so before the examiner asks.

The failure this section exists to prevent

A blocked <script src> produces no server error, no 500, no flash message and no visible gap on the page — the Dashboard renders, its four canvases stay blank, and the only evidence is one line in the browser console that nobody has open. If https://cdnjs.cloudflare.com is missing from script-src, or someone "tidies" the object down to helmet's defaults, that is exactly what a student sees, usually while looking for the bug in public/js/charts.js or in GET /api/dashboard/charts, both of which are fine. The check is one line in the M0 exit criteria: open Dashboard, open DevTools, and confirm the console is empty. The same silent block applies to the font stylesheet, where the only symptom is a different typeface.

6.1.2 Frontend surface — server-rendered, same repo

views/
├─ layout.ejs                   # shell: apartment name + "Juristic Platform" 11px, nav from the role matrix
├─ partials/
│  ├─ nav.ejs                   # generated from the D-36 matrix — no link exists for a forbidden page
│  ├─ flash.ejs                 # one-line Thai success/error strip after a POST-Redirect-GET
│  ├─ status-badge.ejs          # renders data-status="Draft|Unpaid|Paid|Overdue" (D-02, D-21)
│  ├─ money.ejs                 # right-aligned, tabular-nums, "1,790.00"
│  ├─ empty-state.ejs  confirm-dialog.ejs  pagination.ejs  skeleton.ejs
├─ pages/
│  ├─ login.ejs  password.ejs  dashboard.ejs  rooms.ejs  room-detail.ejs  tenants.ejs
│                               # password.ejs = เปลี่ยนรหัสผ่าน, GET/POST /password (FR-49)
│  ├─ readings.ejs  bills.ejs  bill-detail.ejs  payment.ejs  receipt.ejs
│  ├─ payment-history.ejs        # ประวัติการรับชำระ, GET /payments/history — Payment's
│                               #   second tab, so it renders the same tab strip as payment.ejs (FR-25)
│  ├─ announcements.ejs  settings.ejs  users.ejs
│  ├─ reports/1-monthly.ejs  reports/2-arrears.ejs                # D-42 reports 1 and 2
│  ├─ reports/3-room-history.ejs  reports/4-top-usage.ejs         # first two A-14 cut candidates
│  └─ errors/403.ejs  errors/404.ejs  errors/500.ejs
└─ print/
   ├─ receipt.ejs               # A5 portrait, ใบเสร็จรับเงิน, REC-202609-000852 (D-27)
   └─ invoice.ejs               # A5 portrait, ใบแจ้งค่าใช้จ่าย, same layout, no receipt number (D-28)

public/
├─ css/app.css                  # design tokens, layout, components — one file, no preprocessor
│                               #   it IS the designer's tokens.css, copied verbatim in M0 — see below
├─ css/print.css                # @page { size: A5 portrait; margin: 10mm }
├─ js/readings.js               # per-room save + X/Y counter, ~60 lines (D-15)
├─ js/send-drain.js             # the approve result screen's drain loop, 20 at a time (D-19)
├─ js/payment-change.js         # รับเงิน / เงินทอน, screen only, never sent to the server (D-24)
├─ js/announcement-preview.js   # live preview and recipient count (D-35)
├─ js/charts.js                 # Chart.js bootstrap from /api/dashboard/charts (D-41)
└─ js/confirm-dialog.js         # the six confirmation call sites (D-39)
public/css/app.css is the design system, copied — not re-typed

The UI design document is authored against a real stylesheet, and that stylesheet ships with the document set as docs/_shared/tokens.css. It is the single source of the palette, the type scale, the spacing steps, the badge colours and every component class the mockups use. In M0 it is copied verbatim to public/css/app.css — a file copy, not a port, not a "take the tokens and rebuild the components": a divergence between the two is a defect in the app, and the fix is always to copy the file again. There is no build step to compile one into the other (A-1), and there is no second stylesheet; public/css/print.css holds the @page rules and nothing else.

The one thing to know before copying: every rule in that file is scoped under .screen, because the design document renders its mockups inside device frames and must not have the product's styles leak onto its own prose. The app keeps that scope for free — views/layout.ejs wraps the page body in <div class="screen">…</div>, exactly as a device frame does, so the file works unchanged with no find-and-replace and no wrapper stylesheet. Unwrapping the selectors instead would be a one-way edit that makes the next copy a manual merge.

6.2 Development environment

6.2.1 Prerequisites

ToolVersionCheckWhy this exact thing
Node.js24.x LTSnode -v → v24.…--env-file, node:test, fetch and Intl are built in; four dependencies deleted
npmships with Node 24npm -vnpm ci is the reproducible install
MySQL8.4 (8.0.16 minimum)mysql --versionCHECK constraints, stored generated columns, LAST_INSERT_ID(seq+1)

BrowserChrome or Edge, current—D-45 device target; A5 print preview is verified in Chrome

6.2.2 First-time setup — six commands

  1. git clone <repo> && cd juristic-platform
  2. npm ci — installs exactly the locked versions. Never npm install on a fresh clone.
  3. cp .env.example .env then fill it in (§6.2.3). Generate the two secrets with node -e "console.log(require('node:crypto').randomBytes(32).toString('hex'))".
  4. mysql -u root -p -e "CREATE DATABASE juristic CHARACTER SET utf8mb4 COLLATE utf8mb4_0900_ai_ci" — utf8mb4 is not optional; Thai names and LINE message templates live in these tables.
  5. npm run db:reset — drops every table, applies db/schema.sql, then db/seed.sql. Prints 80 rooms · 67 tenants · 54 LINE-linked · rates 18.00/8.00/35.00 · due day 5. Fails loudly if SEED_ADMIN_PASSWORD is unset (D-37).
  6. npm run dev → open http://localhost:3000, log in as admin with your SEED_ADMIN_PASSWORD.
Verify the install with numbers, not with a vibe

Run npm run rehearse. It must print the PRD §28 arithmetic and exit 0:
room 205 · water 1,250 → 1,260 = 10 × 18.00 = 180.00 · electric 5,280 → 5,350 = 70 × 8.00 = 560.00 · common 30.00 × 35.00 = 1,050.00 · total 1,790.00 · receipt REC-<YYYYMM>-000852
If the total is anything other than 1,790.00, stop and fix that before writing another line of code.

.env.test is the same file pointing DATABASE_URL at a scratch database (juristic_test). It is git-ignored, and the integration helper truncates every table between tests, so it must never point at juristic.

6.2.4 npm scripts

linteslint .Before every commit; gate 1 of CI.
