db:resetnpm run db:migrate && npm run db:seedThe one command that recreates the graded demo from empty (D-44).
db:demo-armnode --env-file=.env db/reset.js --file db/demo-reset.sqlRe-arms room 205 for a demo — deletes its current-period readings and bill and resets the counter to 851 (§2.7). Step 0 of the demo script, first action of the §6.3.2 smoke test, and the first thing rehearse does. Idempotent; safe to run twice.
test:e2enpx playwright testTier 4, the seven browser scenarios (test plan §7). Chromium only, one worker, no retries. Needs npx playwright install --with-deps chromium once per machine.
tracenpx playwright show-trace .tmp/playwright-report/trace.zipOpens the trace of the last failed tier-4 run. The only reason tier 4 is debuggable without re-running it by hand.
rehearsenpm run db:demo-arm && node --env-file=.env test/rehearsal.jsArms room 205, then walks the PRD §28 path end to end with LINE test mode ON. Before every push and on the morning of the defence.

6.2.5 Schema changes and seeding

There is no schema_migrations table and no numbered files. A schema change is an edit to db/schema.sql followed by npm run db:reset on every laptop. That is legitimate precisely because there is no production data to preserve — the only database that matters is rebuilt from seed.sql in one command.

SituationProcedure
Adding a column or indexEdit db/schema.sql, edit db/seed.sql if the column needs demo values, npm run db:reset, run npm test, commit both files in the same commit as the code that uses them.
A teammate pulls your schema changeThey run npm run db:reset. Their local data is disposable by design. The PR description says "requires db:reset" on the first line.
Changing the deployed databaseRender Shell → npm run db:migrate → npm run db:seed. Take the dump of §6.3.5 first if the deployed data contains a rehearsal you care about.
Real data must survive a schema changeIt never does in this project. If that changes, the trigger to add a migration runner has arrived — that is a decision, recorded as an ADR amendment, not an afternoon's improvisation.
Preparing a demo or a rehearsal on a seeded databasenpm run db:demo-arm — not db:reset, which would also discard whatever else is on the instance. It applies db/demo-reset.sql (§2.7), leaving 66 approved bills and every other room untouched while room 205 goes back to unread and unbilled. Never apply test/fixtures/reset-205.sql here: it whitelists a test recipient (test plan §2.4.1).

Seed facts a test may rely on (D-44): 80 rooms across 5 floors (101–116 … 501–516), all 30.00 sqm; 67 Occupied, 13 Vacant; 67 Active tenants of which exactly 54 carry a LINE binding; rates 18.00 / 8.00 / 35.00 effective from the first day of the earliest history period; due day 5; for the current period, the other 66 billable rooms each carry an approved Unpaid bill while room 205 is read (readings 812 / 813) and carries the one Draft bill, id 118, total 1790.00 — and no payment and no receipt, which are produced by the demo run (§3.1.10); the receipt counter is left at 851 so room 205's payment issues REC-<current YYYYMM>-000852. Every seed date is computed from CURDATE() — a test asserts 1,790.00, never 5 ก.ย. 2026.

The seed is the finished period, not the demo's starting position. Because room 205 is already read and already has its Draft bill, the demo script's first two actions — save the readings, press ส่งให้นิติตรวจสอบ — have nothing to do until npm run db:demo-arm has run. That is one command, it is step 0 of the demo script, and §2.7 states the file and the reason. A test needing the same state uses test/fixtures/reset-205.sql instead, which is the identical unwinding plus a test-recipient whitelist that must never reach a demo database.

6.2.6 Running locally against LINE

Local development never receives webhooks: the LINE console holds exactly one webhook URL and it belongs to the Render deployment (D-43). Locally, settings.line_test_mode = 1 redirects every outbound message to line_test_user_id, and inbound linking is exercised in two ways — the integration tests post a signed fake message event to POST /line/webhook with a body signed by LINE_CHANNEL_SECRET (the curl fixtures are in §5.8.3), and the deployed instance is used for the one real end-to-end link per milestone. No tunnel, no ngrok, no second webhook URL.

6.3 Deployment

6.3.1 Topology

flowchart TB
  DEV["Student laptop, node --watch, local MySQL 8.4"]
  GH["GitHub main branch, source plus schema.sql and seed.sql"]
  CI["GitHub Actions job verify, lint plus tests"]
  RND["Render free web service, one Node 24 process, fixed HTTPS origin"]
  AIV["Aiven free managed MySQL 8.4, app data and session store"]
  CJ["cron-job.org, two schedules"]
  LOA["LINE Official Account, Messaging API channel"]
  PRN["Office printer, A5 portrait"]

  DEV -->|"git push"| GH
  GH --> CI
  GH -->|"auto deploy on green main"| RND
  RND -->|"TLS, DATABASE_URL"| AIV
  CJ -->|"POST /internal/jobs/daily at 09:00 Asia/Bangkok"| RND
  CJ -->|"GET /healthz every 10 minutes"| RND
  RND -->|"push and multicast"| LOA
  LOA -->|"webhook to the fixed origin"| RND
  RND -->|"browser print dialog"| PRN
  GH -.->|"demo-day fallback, local app on local MySQL from seed.sql"| DEV
Everything on the critical path is free. One origin, one database, two cron schedules, one LINE channel. Secrets exist only as Render environment variables and in a password-protected team note.

6.3.2 Deploy procedure — first time

These seven steps create the environment. The eight checks that prove it is actually correct — timezone, CSP, the forced password change, both cron schedules, and the 1,790.00 — are runbook R1, and they are not optional.

  1. Aiven. Create a free MySQL 8.4 service, region Singapore. Copy the service URI. Create the database: CREATE DATABASE juristic CHARACTER SET utf8mb4.
  2. Render. New → Web Service → connect the GitHub repo → Runtime Node → Build Command npm ci → Start Command npm start → free instance type. Auto-deploy on main.
  3. Environment variables. §6.2.3 names nine application variables. Add exactly seven of them in the Render dashboard, and no others:
    VariableValue
    DATABASE_URLthe Aiven service URI from step 1
    SESSION_SECRET32 random bytes hex, generated once and never rotated during the semester
    LINE_CHANNEL_SECRETLINE console → Basic settings
    LINE_CHANNEL_ACCESS_TOKENLINE console → Messaging API tab
    JOB_TOKEN32-hex string; the identical string is pasted into cron-job.org in step 7
    NODE_ENVproduction
    TZAsia/Bangkok — Render defaults to UTC, so this one is not optional
    Two of the nine are deliberately absent. Do not add PORT — Render injects it, and a hard-coded 3000 makes the deploy unhealthy with "no open port detected" (§6.2.3). Do not add SEED_ADMIN_PASSWORD — it is set transiently in the Render shell for the one seeding run of step 5 and removed afterwards. The four test only variables of §6.2.3 (LINE_MOCK, LINE_OUTBOX_FILE, TEST_BASE_URL, E2E_BASE_URL) are never set on Render at all: LINE_MOCK=1 on the deployed instance means the graded demo sends nothing and every badge still reads ส่งแล้ว.
  4. First deploy. Push to main. Wait for Live. curl https://<service>.onrender.com/healthz must return {"data":{"ok":true,"db":"up"}}.
  5. Seed once. Render Shell → set SEED_ADMIN_PASSWORD for the session → npm run db:reset → confirm the printed counts → log in and change the admin password → remove the variable.
  6. LINE console. Messaging API → Webhook URL = https://<service>.onrender.com/line/webhook → Verify (must be green) → Use webhook ON → auto-reply messages OFF, greeting message ON.
  7. cron-job.org. Two separate jobs. (a) POST https://<service>.onrender.com/internal/jobs/daily, header X-Job-Token: <JOB_TOKEN>, daily at 09:00, timezone Asia/Bangkok. (b) GET https://<service>.onrender.com/healthz, every 10 minutes. These are two jobs, not one — the daily job is not a keep-alive (D-32, D-43).
  8. Smoke test on the deployed instance. Render Shell → npm run db:demo-arm first (§2.7) — without it room 205 is already จดแล้ว with a Draft bill and the smoke test proves nothing. Then log in, open Meter Reading for room 205, save water 1,260 and electricity 5,350, press ส่งให้นิติตรวจสอบ, open Bill Detail, and confirm the total reads 1,790.00. If LINE is part of the check, do §5.8.5's เตรียมสาธิต LINE first: on the shipped settings row the badge turns ส่งแล้ว without a message being sent.

6.3.3 Deploy procedure — every subsequent release

  1. Merge to main only when CI is green (§6.5.3). Render deploys automatically.
  2. If db/schema.sql or db/seed.sql changed: take the dump of §6.3.5, then Render Shell → npm run db:reset.
  3. curl /healthz, then log in and load Dashboard.
  4. Re-verify the webhook URL in the LINE console (runbook R2) — one click, and it is the single line of the runbook that has ever silently killed the linking flow (D-43).
  5. npm run rehearse against the deployed instance's database before any milestone review or the defence.

6.3.4 Secrets

SecretLives inNever lives inRotation
DATABASE_URLRender env vars, local .env, team password notethe repo, a screenshot, a slideOnly if the Aiven service is recreated
SESSION_SECRETRender env varsthe repoNever during the semester — rotating logs everyone out
LINE_CHANNEL_SECRET / _ACCESS_TOKENRender env vars, team password notethe repo, the thesis appendix, a demo slideImmediately if a screenshot leaks one; re-verify the webhook afterwards
JOB_TOKENRender env vars and the cron-job.org job definitionthe repoIf cron-job.org's account is shared outside the team
SEED_ADMIN_PASSWORDeach student's local .env; set transiently in the Render shellthe repo, seed.sql, the READMEChanged at first login on the deployed instance (D-37)

A pre-push habit that costs nothing: git diff --cached | grep -iE "onrender|aiven|channel|secret|password|token" before committing anything that touches configuration.

6.3.5 Backups

ArtifactWhenCommandRestores what
db/schema.sql + db/seed.sqlAlways in the reponpm run db:resetThe entire graded demo from an empty database, in about 20 seconds. This is the real backup.
Milestone dumpBefore every milestone review and the day before the defencemysqldump --single-transaction --set-gtid-purged=OFF juristic | gzip > db/dumps/mysqldump-M4-2026-11-01.sql.gzThe exact state that was demonstrated, including rehearsal payments and receipt numbers
Demo-day laptop copyThe day before the defenceRestore the newest dump into local MySQL and run npm run rehearseThe whole demo with the hosted database unreachable (webhook disabled, test mode ON)
Recovery objective, stated plainly

Acceptable data loss is everything. There is no real tenant data (D-44: all seed content is fictional). The recovery target is time: a destroyed database must be back to a demonstrable state in under 10 minutes, which npm run db:reset meets. The dumps exist only so a rehearsed state — for example a receipt already numbered REC-202611-000852 — can be reproduced exactly. The commands and the verification queries are runbook R6.

6.3.6 Monitoring and logging

SignalWhereWhat good looks likeWhat to do when it is bad
LivenessGET /healthz, pinged every 10 minutes200 {"data":{"ok":true,"db":"up"}}, one SELECT 1cron-job.org emails on failure; check Render logs, then Aiven status
Request logRender logs, JSON lines from lib/log.js: {"t":"2026-09-03T14:30:02+07:00","lvl":"info","msg":"POST /api/bills/approve","status":200,"ms":3120}Approve under 5 s (D-45 target 4)Grep "lvl":"error"; the stack is on stdout and never in the browser
Daily jobjob_runs table, one row per dayA row for every date with finished_at set and a summary_jsonA missing row means the cron never authenticated — check JOB_TOKEN, then runbook R8
Delivery healthnotifications tableSELECT status, COUNT(*) FROM notifications WHERE send_date = CURDATE() GROUP BY status — failed rows should be zeroRows with attempt_count = 3 need the Admin's per-bill ส่งแจ้งเตือนอีกครั้ง, which is never capped (D-30)
LINE quotaSettings page tile: ส่งข้อความเดือนนี้: {N}/500Below 450 (warn colour above)Keep test mode ON; announcements go by multicast, never one push per tenant (D-34)
Audit trailaudit_log, indexed on (bill_id, created_at)Every approve, payment and void has a row written inside the same transaction (D-38)A money change with no audit row is a defect, not a logging gap

There is no APM, no error tracker and no log aggregation. Render keeps the last logs, MySQL keeps job_runs, notifications and audit_log, and those three tables answer every operational question this system can be asked.

6.4 Implementation plan

6.4.1 Shape of the semester

4 students × 8 h/week × 14 weeks = 448 h ≈ 56 person-days  ·  1 person-day = 8 focused hours  ·  team velocity = 4 person-days per calendar week

Weeks run Monday to Sunday from 2026-09-07 to 2026-12-13. Estimates below are in person-days. They now total 61 against a capacity of 56 — the plan carries a declared five-day overdraft, and stating it here is the point of the exercise.

Where the extra five person-days came from, and how they are paid for

The first draft of this plan totalled 56 and named only three requirements across the whole of §6.4, which made "the milestone's scope" undefined and let five requirements fall through the gaps entirely. Giving every milestone an explicit FR list (§6.4.2) surfaced work that was never estimated:

AddedWhereEffort
FR-49 เปลี่ยนรหัสผ่าน — the screen D-37's "first login forces a change" is realised byM0+0.5
FR-26 ดูประวัติมิเตอร์ (a room's reading history) and FR-44 the two soft usage flagsM2+1
FR-25 ประวัติการรับชำระ — GET /payments/history, which had no milestone at allM4+1
FR-32's per-bill delivery badges on Monthly Billing and FR-40's ส่งข้อความเดือนนี้: {N}/500 lineM5+0.5
Tier 4 — the seven Playwright scenarios admitted by the A-9 amendment (§6.5.1)M8+2

The overdraft is paid out of A-14, in its stated order, and the order already covers it. Tier 4 is ahead of every A-14 item (2 pd); A-14 cut 1, report 4 ห้องที่ใช้น้ำ/ไฟสูงสุด, is about 1 pd; cut 2, CSV on all four reports, about 1.5; cut 3, two of the four charts, about 1.5. Any two of those four clear it. The rule stands unchanged: a cut that retires PRD-mandated scope is declared to the advisor before it is taken.

A three-student team has 42 person-days and is 19 short, not 14. It takes tier 4 and A-14 cuts 1–3 at the start of M7 — that is 6 of the 19 — and the remaining 13 come out of scope the advisor is told about in week 12, not out of the demo path, which is never cut.

M7Dashboard, four reports, CSVW124 pdM3 (M6 for the notification tile)FR-28 · FR-29 · FR-30 · FR-43Dashboard tiles for the current period, the common-area total reading 69,300.00 on a fresh seed and 70,350.00 after room 205 is approved, four Chart.js charts, arrears report exporting a UTF-8 CSV that opens in Excel with Thai intact.

Total61 pdcapacity 5649 of 49 requirements, each in exactly one milestone. No FR appears twice and none is unassigned — that is the property the column exists to make checkable, and the reason the total moved from 56 (§6.4.1).
This column is what makes the test plan's per-milestone exit criterion executable

The test plan's exit criterion reads "every FR in the milestone's scope has at least one test that names its id, and the traceability grep reports zero uncovered FRs for that milestone" (test plan §2.7). Until this column existed, the milestone's scope named nothing: §6.4 mentioned three requirement ids in total and five requirements — FR-25, FR-26, FR-32, FR-40 and FR-44 — appeared in no milestone at all, which is how the criterion could be signed off while a screen nobody had built stayed unbuilt. The grep is now literally runnable: take the row's FR list, grep the test titles for each id, and a milestone with an empty result does not exit.

Two entries are deliberately split rather than duplicated. FR-02 sits in M0, where the requireRole middleware and the Thai 403 page are written; M8 adds the exhaustive per-route test that asserts 403 and that no amount reaches a staff response body, and M8's row names that as a hardening pass rather than as a second owner. FR-30 sits in M7 with the charts, but its per-room 12-period table shares its query with FR-26's reading history from M2 — build the query once in M2, render the second view in M7.

6.4.3 Tasks per milestone

M0 · Walking skeleton and deployment — W1–W2, 8.5 pd
  • Create the repo, package.json with engines: ">=24 <25", ESLint + Prettier configs, .env.example, .gitignore.
  • server.js with the full middleware order: helmet → static → session → csrf → requireAuth → requireRole → zod → route → middleware/errors.js.
  • db/pool.js with timezone: '+07:00', dateStrings: true, decimalNumbers off; db/tx.js; db/reset.js.
  • db/schema.sql for users, settings, sessions only — the rest arrives with its milestone.
  • Login, logout, bcryptjs cost 10, MySQL session store, rate limiter, app.set('trust proxy', 1) (D-37).
  • lib/log.js, lib/errors.js, middleware/csrf.js + its unit test, the Thai 403/404/500 pages.
  • GET/POST /password and views/pages/password.ejs (FR-49) — three fields, bcrypt re-hash at cost 10, the session kept, the two verbatim Thai messages รหัสผ่านต้องมีอย่างน้อย 8 ตัวอักษร and รหัสผ่านไม่ตรงกัน, plus the dismissible Dashboard banner คุณยังใช้รหัสผ่านเริ่มต้นอยู่ — กรุณาเปลี่ยนรหัสผ่าน. The link lives in the account block of both navigations, never as a fourth staff tab (UI design §4). This is how D-37's "first login forces a change" ships (§3.7 item 8); it is not a self-service reset.
  • views/layout.ejs + public/css/app.css tokens; empty Dashboard shell.
  • Register the LINE Official Account and the Messaging API channel now (R-01) — no code depends on it yet, and approval is the one thing money cannot accelerate in week 9.
  • Render + Aiven + both cron-job.org schedules live; .github/workflows/ci.yml green.
M1 · Rooms, tenants, settings, rates, seed — W3–W4, 8 pd
  • Schema for rooms, tenants (with the active_room_id generated column and its unique key), rates, counters, audit_log.
  • Rooms list and Room detail; create/edit; deactivate instead of delete; floor entered, never parsed from room_code (D-47).
  • POST /rooms/:id/activate — เปิดใช้งานอีกครั้ง, the counterpart of deactivate (§3.2.3, §3.3.3). Sets is_active = 1 and writes ROOM_ACTIVATE on the same connection; no reason field; 303 back to Room detail. Build it in the same pass as deactivate — the warn banner that offers the button is already on the page, and shipping only half the pair is what makes a deactivated room unrecoverable without SQL. Add ROOM_ACTIVATE to the audit_log.action enum in db/schema.sql while that table is being written (§1.8.1, §2.4).
  • Tenants: create, end tenancy with a Thai reason, the one-Active-tenant rule proven by the database not by a service (D-10).
  • Settings: apartment identity, due day with CHECK (due_day BETWEEN 1 AND 28), rate insert that never UPDATEs (D-17).
  • Users page, Admin only, password reset by Admin (D-46).
  • db/seed.sql complete: 80 rooms, 67 tenants, 54 LINE-linked, room 312's completed tenancy, every date from CURDATE() (D-44).
  • lib/money.js, lib/datetime.js, lib/thaiBaht.js with their unit tests — including 1,790.00 → หนึ่งพันเจ็ดร้อยเก้าสิบบาทถ้วน.
M2 · Meter Reading — W5, 5 pd
  • Schema for meter_readings with uq_readings(room_id, period, utility_type), CHECK (units >= 0), previous_period VARCHAR(8).
  • Previous-reading resolution: strictly earlier period, order by period DESC, fall back to the room baseline with previous_period = 'baseline' (D-06, D-14).
  • Meter Reading page grouped by floor, phone-first, 44 px tap targets, numeric keypad, X/Y counter (D-15, D-45 target 1).
  • POST /api/readings as an UPSERT; a double submit changes nothing (D-12).
  • Hard block on current below previous with the Thai message and the fields payload; meter-replacement branch and its two validations (D-13).
  • Room reading history (FR-26) — the 12-period table on Room detail: previous, current and units per utility per period, 'baseline' shown as the source for the first period, and a skipped month rendered as a gap rather than a zero row. M1 built Room detail before any reading existed, so this is the pass that fills it in; the same query is reused by FR-30's per-room view in M7, so write it once here.
  • The two soft usage flags (FR-44) — ใช้สูงผิดปกติ when the previous period's usage for that utility was ≥ 10 units and the current usage exceeds 3× it, ไม่มีการใช้งาน on 0 units for a room with an Active tenant. Neither ever blocks a save, a generation run or an approval. They are A-14 cut 7, so they are built plainly and are the cheapest thing on this list to delete.
  • Write the PRD §28 arithmetic test now, red: 1,250→1,260 = 10 units, 5,280→5,350 = 70 units. It turns green in M3 when the item amounts exist. The period comes from currentPeriod(), never from a literal (§6.5.2).
M3 · Billing — W6–W7, 8 pd
  • Schema for bills (uq_bills_room_period, three snapshot columns) and bill_items (uq_bill_items(bill_id, item_type)).
  • Generation: one transaction per room, three items always, amount = ROUND(quantity * unit_price, 2) in SQL, total = SQL SUM of rounded items (D-16, D-18).
  • Rate resolution keyed to the period's first day, not to the click (D-17).
  • Monthly Billing list, Bill Detail with the audit timeline, data-status on every status element.
  • Approve: UPDATE … WHERE status='Draft', due_date = MAX(due day, approval + 3 days) clamped to month end (D-20); bulk approve as one transaction returning in ≤ 5 s (D-19).
  • Revert to Draft: Admin only, reason ≥ 10 characters, refused when a later bill exists for the room (D-22).
  • Display status helper in both SQL and JS, agreeing on the same fixtures (D-21).
  • Invoice A5 print view (D-28).
  • Green: 10 × 18.00 = 180.00, 70 × 8.00 = 560.00, 30.00 × 35.00 = 1,050.00, total 1,790.00.
M4 · Payment and receipt — W8, 5 pd
  • Schema for payments (active_bill_id generated + unique) and receipts (uq_receipts(payment_id), uq_receipts(receipt_no)).
  • Confirm cash: conditional status update, 0 rows → 409 บิลนี้ถูกชำระไปแล้ว; receipt number from counters via ON DUPLICATE KEY UPDATE seq = LAST_INSERT_ID(seq + 1) (D-26).
  • Void payment: Admin only, reason required, payment and receipt marked voided and never deleted (D-25).
  • Receipt A5 print view with the letterhead from Settings, Thai words, print_count increment before window.print() (D-27).
  • Change calculator on screen only, never posted (D-24).
  • GET /payments/history — ประวัติการรับชำระ (FR-25), Payment's second tab and the views/pages/payment-history.ejs of §6.1.2: every bill for a room with its period, total, display status, payment date and receipt number, including bills belonging to a previous now-Inactive tenant, and a voided payment still listed and stamped ยกเลิก. Selection is by payment date, not by billing period — the รอบบิล column is what makes the difference visible (§3.3.12, D-41). Filters ?period=&q=&filter=all|live|voided&room_id=; the summary line excludes voided rows; the row query is §4.13.3's MAX(p2.id) join and never active_bill_id, or the voided payment vanishes. The route is in the catalogue at §3.2.7, which closes the test plan's open item #10.
  • Integration tests: double confirm → one payment, one receipt, second call 409; void then re-pay → a new number, the old one still taken.
M5 · LINE — W9–W10, 8.5 pd
  • Schema for link_codes, line_followers, notifications with the dedupe_key generated column and its unique key.
  • POST /line/webhook: express.raw before the JSON parser, validateSignature, ACK 200 before any work, SDK middleware() deliberately not used (D-29).
  • Link codes: 6 characters from the unambiguous alphabet, 7 days, single use; printable slip with the OA QR code.
  • services/notify.js: enqueue one row per recipient, drain at most 20 per call under GET_LOCK('notif_drain', 0), retry below attempt_count = 3, reuse the stored X-Line-Retry-Key.
  • Test mode redirect to line_test_user_id, default ON in every environment (D-34).
  • lib/templates.js: the seven Thai templates, braced placeholders only (D-31).
  • Approve result screen with the drain loop; PAID message after payment.
  • Per-bill delivery badges on Monthly Billing and Bill Detail (FR-32) — D-30's three labels and no fourth: ส่งแล้ว, ยังไม่ได้ส่ง — ไม่มี LINE, ส่งไม่สำเร็จ with last_error on hover; a queued row and a Draft bill both render — (§5.6.7). Bill Detail also gets the delivery panel and the uncapped ส่งแจ้งเตือนอีกครั้ง button.
  • Settings' quota line (FR-40) — ส่งข้อความเดือนนี้: {N}/500 counting notifications rows with status='sent' in the current month, warn colour above 450, throttling nothing — beside the โหมดทดสอบ LINE and บัญชีรับข้อความทดสอบ controls that §5.8.5's demo preparation reads.
  • Spike on day one of M5: confirm GET_LOCK works on the managed MySQL, else switch to FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED (R-09).
  • Print the A5 receipt on the real office printer this week — week 9 is the deadline in A-13.5. The receipt view has existed since M4, and paper is the one thing no test covers (R-06).
M6 · Job, reminders, announcements — W11, 4 pd
  • Schema for job_runs (uq_job_runs(job_name, run_date)) and announcements.
  • POST /internal/jobs/daily with X-Job-Token; PRE_DUE at due − 2 days; OVERDUE at 1, 8 and 15 days past due; at most four automatic messages per bill for its whole life (D-33).
  • Bills whose latest payment was voided are excluded from automatic sends (D-25).
  • The manual button รันงานแจ้งเตือนตอนนี้ using job_name='daily_manual' and skipping the job_runs guard (D-32).
  • Announcements: compose, live preview, recipient count, multicast in chunks of ≤ 500, one notifications row per recipient, immutable once sent (D-35).
  • Integration test: the job twice in one day is a no-op; the manual button afterwards still sends nothing twice.
M7 · Dashboard and reports — W12, 4 pd
  • Dashboard tiles scoped to the selected period, money tiles aggregating status IN ('Unpaid','Paid') (D-41).
  • GET /api/dashboard/charts?period=2026-09 and four Chart.js charts from cdnjs.
  • Reports 1–4 and ?format=csv through lib/csv.js, UTF-8 with BOM so Excel shows Thai (D-42).
  • Verify the seed reproduces 66 × 30.00 × 35.00 = 69,300.00 on the common-area tile before the demo approval, and 67 × 30.00 × 35.00 = 70,350.00 immediately after room 205 is approved; do not expect PRD §16's 18,500 or 62,500 — they are not reproducible at these rates (D-44).
  • Any list page under 2 seconds against the full seed of ~800 bills (D-45 target 2) — add the missing index rather than a cache.
M8 · Hardening and rehearsal — W13–W14, 10 pd
  • Re-print the A5 receipt and the A5 invoice on the office printer after the final CSS pass — the first real print happened in M5, week 9; fix margins in public/css/print.css only (A-13.5).
  • Every one of the five D-45 numbers measured with a stopwatch against the seed and written down.
  • One integration test per staff-forbidden route asserting 403 and that no amount appears anywhere in the response body (D-36).
  • Empty states, the D-23 missing-readings warn banner, the six confirmation dialogs (D-39).
  • Tier 4, 2 person-days (A-9 amendment, §6.5.1) — playwright.config.ts, the nine-line tsconfig.json scoped to test/e2e/, the seven specs of test plan §7, and the third CI job. Chromium only, workers: 1, retries: 0. This is first in the cut order: if week 13 is short, it goes before any A-14 item.
  • test/rehearsal.js run daily for the last week; the milestone dump committed. It runs db:demo-arm first, so a rehearsal never depends on the state the previous rehearsal left (§2.7).
  • Demo-day fallback rehearsed once for real: hosted database unplugged, local app on a restored dump, webhook disabled, test mode ON.
  • Document set finalised; the defence walkthrough timed against the PRD §26 flow.

The demo path owns eight of the fourteen weeks. Everything after 15 November is either supporting scope that A-14 allows cutting, or hardening that A-14 forbids cutting.

6.4.5 The order that gets the PRD §28 demo working earliest

The plan above is already that order, but the reason is worth stating so nobody "optimises" it. Each milestone from M0 to M5 adds exactly one link in the §28 chain, and no milestone before M5 exists to make a different screen prettier.

By the end ofDateOf the PRD §28 chain, this much runs
M14 OctRoom 205 exists at 30.00 sqm with one Active tenant and rates 18.00 / 8.00 / 35.00.
M211 OctWater 1,250 → 1,260 = 10 units; electricity 5,280 → 5,350 = 70 units.
M325 Oct180.00 + 560.00 + 1,050.00 = 1,790.00, approved, due date frozen.
M41 NovCash confirmed, Paid, receipt REC-<YYYYMM>-000852 printed on A5. Six weeks before the defence, the whole chain runs except the two LINE messages.
M515 NovThe complete chain including the BILL message ending ยอดรวม 1,790 บาท and the PAID message ยืนยันการชำระเงิน.
Two sequencing rules that are easy to get wrong
  • Seed room 205 in M1, not in M8. The 1,790.00 assertion must be runnable the week the arithmetic is written. A seed built at the end is a seed nobody has tested against.
  • Register the LINE channel in M0. It is the only dependency in this project with an external waiting time, and it blocks M5 completely. Everything else can be started on any Monday.

6.4.6 Team split

RoleOwnsPrimary milestonesPairs with
Billing ownerservices/billing.js, services/payment.js, lib/money.js, lib/thaiBaht.js, lib/billStatus.jsM3, M4Data owner on schema.sql
Integrations ownerroutes/line.js, services/notify.js, services/linking.js, services/jobs.js, lib/templates.jsM5, M6Billing owner on the approve → enqueue seam
UI ownerviews/, public/css/, public/js/, both print views, Chart.jsM2, M7Everyone — every milestone ends in a screen
Data & QA ownerdb/schema.sql, db/seed.sql, services/reports.js, test/, CIM1, M7, M8Billing owner on the money tests

Three-student fallback: the Data & QA role is dissolved — schema.sql and seed.sql go to the Billing owner, services/reports.js to the UI owner, and tests are written by whoever wrote the code with a mandatory second reviewer. Effort drops to 42 person-days, so A-14 cuts 1–3 (report 4, all CSV exports, two of the four charts) are taken at the start of M7 rather than debated in week 14. Cuts 1 and 2 retire PRD-mandated scope — PRD §17's top-usage report, FR-43 and half of FR-29 — and are declared to the advisor in week 12 (A-14's Retires column).

No module on the §28 demo path has a single owner. Every one of billing.js, payment.js, notify.js and schema.sql has a named second reader who has run its tests locally — the mitigation for R-07 that costs nothing while everyone is healthy.

6.5 Quality strategy

6.5.1 Four tiers

TierRunnerCoversExample assertionRuns
Unit — no databasenpm run test:unitEvery calculation and format: thaiBaht(), previous-reading resolution over fixtures, meter-replacement units, item rounding, rate resolution by period, the MAX(due day, approval + 3) clamp, billStatus(), receipt-number formatting, the CSRF compareassert.equal(thaiBaht('1790.00'), 'หนึ่งพันเจ็ดร้อยเก้าสิบบาทถ้วน')On save, in about a second
Integration — scratch schema, real HTTPnpm run test:intThe races that lose money: double confirm, void then re-pay, generation twice, approve twice, the daily job twice, concurrent receipt allocation, the dedupe key, revert with a later bill, and 403 + no-amount for every staff-forbidden routeA second POST /payments returns 409 with error.code = "BILL_ALREADY_PAID"; SELECT COUNT(*) FROM receipts is still 1Before every push, in CI
Rehearsal — end to end on the seednpm run rehearseArms room 205 with db:demo-arm first (§2.7 — the seed ships it read and billed), then walks the PRD §28 path with LINE test mode ON: read room 205 → generate → approve → drain → confirm payment → receipt → both notification rowsTotal is '1790.00'; two notifications rows exist with status='sent' — via §5.6.5's swallow branch while line_test_user_id is NULL, so nothing reaches LINE; the period is currentPeriod(), never a literal — the arithmetic is asserted, the calendar never is (D-44)Before every push and on the morning of the defence
Browser E2E — the screens an examiner sees A-9 amendment, grantednpm run test:e2eThe seven scenarios that only a rendered page can prove: the six D-39 confirmation dialogs, the reading round at 360 px, the Thai 403 page, the sticky X/Y counter, the derived Overdue badge, the approve result screen's drain loop, and the A5 receipt as it prints. Playwright, Chromium only, workers: 1, retries: 0, devDependencies — nothing reaches the runtime and the deployed process is byte-identicalScenario E2E-01 walks the PRD §28 path in a browser and asserts the outbox file and the notifications rows together (test plan §7.8)In CI on main and every pull request

There is no coverage gate. What is enforced is the list above: a test missing from that list is a gap, a coverage percentage is a number.

ADR A-9 amendment — Playwright is admitted as tier 4, dev-only. granted

ADR §1 lists Playwright under explicitly rejected dependencies and A-9 rejects it by name ("a browser download and a week of setup in a semester that needs the twelfth screen"). The test plan requested a narrower amendment (test plan §2.2) and it is granted here, on these terms, which are binding:

  • Scope. One devDependency (@playwright/test), one playwright.config.ts, one nine-line tsconfig.json scoped to test/e2e/, one test/e2e/ folder of seven specs, and one CI job. The ten runtime dependencies of the Stack table are untouched, npm start and the Render build command are unchanged, and A-1's "no build step" still holds for the application — Playwright compiles its own specs and the app never sees a toolchain.
  • Cost, and where it comes from. 2 person-days, added to M8, taking the semester from 56 to 58 committed person-days (§6.4.1). It is not free and is not pretended to be.
  • Cut order. Tier 4 is cut before item (1) of A-14, because it ships no screen. Tiers 1–3 are the safety net and A-14 already protects tier 3. A three-student team cuts it at the start of M7 without discussion.
  • What does not change. No coverage gate, no snapshot testing, no component tests, no browser other than Chromium, no test dependency in tiers 1–3, and no secret in any tier: CI sets LINE_MOCK=1 and the literal string ci-only-not-a-secret.

This closes the test plan's open item §9.3 #9. The identifiers it introduces — playwright.config.ts, tsconfig.json, test/e2e/, test/fixtures/, the scripts test:e2e and trace, the database juristic_e2e, the file .env.e2e, and the variables LINE_OUTBOX_FILE, E2E_BASE_URL and TEST_BASE_URL — are listed in §6.1.1, §6.2.3 and §6.2.4 and are reused verbatim by the test plan.

6.5.2 The one test that may never go red

import { currentPeriod } from '../../lib/datetime.js';

test('PRD 28 demo arithmetic', async () => {
  const period = currentPeriod();                      // NEVER the literal '2026-09'
  const bill = await generateFor({ room: '205', period });
  assert.equal(bill.items.water.quantity, '10.00');    // 1,260 - 1,250, DECIMAL(10,2)
  assert.equal(bill.items.water.amount, '180.00');     // 10 x 18.00
  assert.equal(bill.items.electric.quantity, '70.00'); // 5,350 - 5,280, DECIMAL(10,2)
  assert.equal(bill.items.electric.amount, '560.00');  // 70 x 8.00
  assert.equal(bill.items.common.amount, '1050.00');   // 30.00 x 35.00
  assert.equal(bill.total, '1790.00');                 // sum of the printed lines
});

The period is derived, never written down. db/seed.sql builds its current period from DATE_FORMAT(CURDATE(),'%Y-%m') (D-44), so a freshly seeded database on 1 October 2026 holds no 2026-09 readings for room 205 at all and a hard-coded literal turns this test red three weeks into M4 — for a calendar reason, in the one test whose whole purpose is arithmetic. The rule is the one this document states twice elsewhere: the arithmetic is asserted, the calendar never is. It applies to every runnable assertion in the repository, including test/rehearsal.js (§6.5.1) and the tier-4 oracles; §3.3's payload examples show 2026-09 because they are documentation, and §3.1.10 says so.

Every value is a string, because mysql2 returns DECIMAL as a string and no amount ever passes through a JavaScript float (D-16). A test that compares to a number is a test that will pass while the receipt prints 1789.9999999999998. The quantities carry their two decimal places because bill_items.quantity is DECIMAL(10,2) (§2.3.8) — the wire payload of §3.3.8 shows the same "10.00" and "70.00". Asserting '10' would fail on the first run.

6.5.3 Continuous integration

There is exactly one .github/workflows/ci.yml, and the test plan owns it

The authoritative file is test plan §2.11. An earlier draft of this section carried a second, single-job version of the same path; it is deleted rather than reconciled, because two files both presented as .github/workflows/ci.yml is worse than either of them. Nothing is duplicated here: to change CI, change test plan §2.11.

What this specification requires of that file, and what a reviewer checks it against:

#GateJobFails when
1npm ci installs from the committed lock fileverifyA dependency was added with npm install and the lock file was not committed
2npm run lintverifyESLint, in under a minute, with no database
3npm run test:unitverifyThe arithmetic is wrong, so no higher tier's result would mean anything
4npm run db:migrate — db/schema.sql applies to an empty databaseapiA schema that only works because someone's laptop still has the old column
5npm run db:seed — db/seed.sql rebuilds the graded demoapiThe seed drifted from the schema, which is the D-44 deliverable breaking silently
6npm run test:int — the races that lose moneyapiDouble confirm, generation twice, approve twice, the dedupe key, a staff route leaking an amount
7npx playwright test — the seven browser scenariose2eA screen disagrees with the database (tier 4, granted under the A-9 amendment in §6.5.1)
Two ordering rules the workflow must satisfy, and why the deleted version did not
  1. Both env files are written immediately after npm ci, before any npm script runs. db:migrate and db:seed are node --env-file=.env … and test:int is node --test --env-file=.env.test …; both files are git-ignored (§6.1.1), so a job that writes only .env.test, and writes it after db:migrate, exits ENOENT on the schema gate and never reaches the tests it claims to run. That was the defect in the deleted version, and the test plan records it as an S2.
  2. The api job starts the server itself. Tier 2 drives the app over HTTP with built-in fetch (§6.5.1), so without node --env-file=.env.test server.js & and a port wait, every tier-2 case fails ECONNREFUSED and gate 6 silently tests nothing. The e2e job does not need this: playwright.config.ts declares its own webServer.

Each job gets its own mysql:8.4 service container — juristic_test for api, juristic_e2e for e2e — so neither can leave state for the other. No secret appears anywhere: every value is the literal string ci-only-not-a-secret, which is safe precisely because LINE_MOCK=1 means no credential is used against a real endpoint (R-12).

M0's exit condition is unchanged and now unambiguous: ".github/workflows/ci.yml green in week 2" means the file of test plan §2.11, with jobs verify and api green. The e2e job joins them in M8, when tier 4 is written; until then it is present and skipped by an empty test/e2e/, not absent.

6.5.4 Definition of done

A task is done when every line below is true. Not "mostly", and not "the happy path".

  • The feature works against db/seed.sql, not against three rows typed by hand.
  • Every visible string is Thai; every route, filename and data-status value is the English identifier (D-02).
  • Money is formatted by lib/money.js and computed in SQL. No *, + or toFixed touches an amount in JavaScript.
  • The failure path renders: 422 shows the Thai inline message with the typed values preserved; 403 shows the Thai page with the button back to จดมิเตอร์ (D-40).
  • Anything that changes money or bill state writes its audit_log row on the same connection, inside the same transaction (D-38).
  • A unit test exists for any calculation, and an integration test exists for any state transition.
  • npm run lint and npm test are green locally, and CI is green on the branch.
  • The PR description names the decision ids it implements and says "requires db:reset" when the schema or seed changed.
  • A second student has pulled the branch, run npm run db:reset and clicked the feature once.
  • It introduces nothing from the PRD §24 exclusion list — no rent, no lease, no gateway, no slip, no QR, no IoT, no AI.

Identifiers introduced by this section

These names are new here and sibling sections must reuse them exactly: the npm scripts dev, start, lint, format, test, test:unit, test:int, test:e2e, trace, db:migrate, db:seed, db:reset, db:demo-arm, rehearse; the module services/records.js (Rooms, Tenants, Users and Settings CRUD — the ADR names six services, and the layering rule leaves these four pages without one); the files db/reset.js, test/rehearsal.js, test/helpers/db.js, test/helpers/client.js, test/helpers/fakeLine.js, test/helpers/lineWebhook.js, db/demo-reset.sql, views/pages/password.ejs, playwright.config.ts, tsconfig.json, the directories test/e2e/ and test/fixtures/, .github/workflows/ci.yml (jobs verify, api, e2e — the file itself is test plan §2.11), .nvmrc, .prettierrc.json, .env.test, .env.e2e; the directory db/dumps/ with filenames mysqldump-<milestone>-<YYYY-MM-DD>.sql.gz; the environment variables PORT, LINE_OUTBOX_FILE, TEST_BASE_URL and E2E_BASE_URL (the other eight are ADR §2.2); the requirement id FR-49; the database juristic_e2e; the milestone ids M0–M8; the team roles Billing owner, Integrations owner, UI owner, Data & QA owner; the risk ids R-01–R-13; the local database names juristic and juristic_test; the branch convention <milestone>/<slug>; and the report view filenames reports/1-monthly.ejs, reports/2-arrears.ejs, reports/3-room-history.ejs, reports/4-top-usage.ejs.

Operations Runbook

Four documents in this set delegate a procedure to "the runbook". This is it. Every item below is a thing that is done to a running deployment, by a person, at a keyboard, usually under time pressure — so each one states the trigger, the commands verbatim, and the observation that proves it worked. Nothing here is a design decision; every rule it applies is decided somewhere else in this document and is cited.

Three rules that apply to every procedure on this page
  • Never on demo morning. Rotating a secret, re-seeding, or re-verifying a webhook is a normal Tuesday activity and a catastrophic Friday one. The only procedure written to be run on the day of the defence is the demo-day fallback.
  • Changing a Render environment variable restarts the service. Sessions survive (they are rows in MySQL), but the free instance then takes about 50 seconds to answer its first request. Budget for it; do not conclude the deploy is broken.
  • Every SQL statement here is run against the deployed database. Read the WHERE clause out loud before pressing return. Only two procedures on this page write to application tables at all — the token recovery and the lost-Admin reset — and both say so.
  • mysql "$DATABASE_URL" below is shorthand. The classic MySQL client takes flags, not a connection URI, and DATABASE_URL is the only form the deployment has. Split it once per shell, then every command on this page works as written: 
    eval "$(node -e 'const u = new URL(process.env.DATABASE_URL); console.log(
  `DBH=${u.hostname}; DBP=${u.port || 3306}; DBU=${u.username};`,
  `DBW=${decodeURIComponent(u.password)}; DBN=${u.pathname.slice(1)}`)')"

mysql -h"$DBH" -P"$DBP" -u"$DBU" -p"$DBW" --ssl-mode=REQUIRED "$DBN" -e "SELECT 1"
    Read mysql "$DATABASE_URL" -e "…" anywhere below as that same flag list. Node's own db/pool.js parses the URI directly, which is why the application never needs this.

R1. First deploy and the post-deploy checks

Trigger: the first deploy of the semester, or a rebuild of the Render service from scratch. The seven creation steps are §6.3.2; what follows is the acceptance test for them, and it is the only evidence that the environment of §6.2.3 is actually right rather than merely typed.

  1. Health. curl -sS https://<service>.onrender.com/healthz
    Expected: {"data":{"ok":true,"db":"up"}}. A 500 here is DATABASE_URL or an Aiven service still provisioning — the app answers healthz the moment it can run one SELECT 1, so nothing else is worth checking until this is green. The first request after an idle period takes about 50 seconds on the free instance; that is the instance waking, not a failure.
  2. Timezone, from the database's own mouth. Render Shell →
    node --env-file=/dev/null -e "console.log(new Date().toString())" must print GMT+0700, and
    mysql "$DATABASE_URL" -e "SELECT NOW(), @@session.time_zone" must show Bangkok local time and +07:00.
    Why it is step 2 and not step 9: a wrong TZ makes a bill due 2026-09-05 render เกินกำหนด seven hours early, and every symptom of that appears days later in someone else's feature (§6.2.3, D-03).
  3. The login page, with DevTools open. Load /login. The Thai text renders in Noto Sans Thai and the browser console is empty. A console line naming cdnjs.cloudflare.com or fonts.googleapis.com means the Content-Security-Policy is wrong — see §6.1.1's CSP block, and fix it now, because its only later symptom is an empty Dashboard chart that looks like a Chart.js bug.
  4. Seed once, then log in. Render Shell → set SEED_ADMIN_PASSWORD for that shell session only → npm run db:reset → confirm the printed line 80 rooms · 67 tenants · 54 LINE-linked · rates 18.00/8.00/35.00 · due day 5. Log in as admin at /login; a successful login lands on /dashboard (§3.3.1).
  5. Force the password change — this is the step D-37 is realised by. There is no must_change_password column and no forced redirect (§3.7 item 8); the mechanism is the Dashboard banner คุณยังใช้รหัสผ่านเริ่มต้นอยู่ — กรุณาเปลี่ยนรหัสผ่าน plus this step. Open GET /password, submit POST /password with the seeded password as current_password and a new one of at least 8 characters twice. Expected: the flash strip confirms, the session survives (no re-login), and the banner is gone. Then remove SEED_ADMIN_PASSWORD from the Render environment — while it exists, the value that was hashed into the database is sitting in a dashboard anyone with repo access can read.
  6. Create the second Admin now. /users → create a second admin account for a different student. This is not tidiness: it is the entire supported recovery path for a lost password (D-46), and building it later means building it from R4 instead.
  7. Both schedules, confirmed by their effects. After the first 09:00 Asia/Bangkok has passed:
    mysql "$DATABASE_URL" -e "SELECT id, job_name, run_date, finished_at FROM job_runs ORDER BY id DESC LIMIT 5"
    Expected: one daily row for the date, finished_at not NULL. No row at all means the cron never authenticated — see R8. The 10-minute /healthz ping is a separate schedule and is confirmed by the instance answering quickly rather than after 50 seconds (D-32, D-43).
  8. The arithmetic, on the deployed instance. Render Shell → npm run db:demo-arm, then walk room 205 through จดมิเตอร์ → ส่งให้นิติตรวจสอบ → Bill Detail and read 1,790.00 (§6.3.2 step 8). A deploy that is green on every check above and wrong on this one is not deployed.

R2. Webhook re-verification and credential rotation

Trigger: any redeploy that changed the service URL, a recreated Render service, a rotated channel secret or access token, and — as a habit — the check in §6.3.3 step 4 before every milestone review. A channel holds exactly one webhook URL (§5.8.4), so this is a one-field procedure with a disproportionate failure mode: linking stops working and nothing anywhere reports it, because a rejected webhook call is LINE's problem, not the app's.

Re-verifying after a deploy or a URL change

  1. LINE Developers Console → the Messaging API channel → Messaging API tab → Webhook URL. It must read https://<service>.onrender.com/line/webhook, with the current host and no trailing slash.
  2. Press Verify. Expected: the green Success toast. A 401 here is a LINE_CHANNEL_SECRET mismatch between the console and Render; a timeout is usually the free instance asleep — press Verify a second time after curl /healthz has woken it.
  3. Confirm Use webhook is still Enabled, Response mode is still Bot, and Auto-reply messages is still Disabled (§5.2.1). Recreating a channel resets all three, and Chat mode silently stops the webhook firing at all.
  4. Prove it end to end rather than trusting the toast: from any shell with LINE_CHANNEL_SECRET exported, send the signed follow event of §5.8.3 at the deployed origin. Expected: HTTP/1.1 200 OK and one new row in line_followers.

Rotating the channel secret

Rotate immediately if a secret reaches a screenshot, a slide, a commit or the thesis appendix (§6.3.4). Order matters, because between the console issuing a new secret and Render restarting with it, every webhook call answers 401 and linking is silently dead.

  1. Console → Basic settings → Issue a new channel secret. Copy it.
  2. Render → Environment → LINE_CHANNEL_SECRET → paste → Save. The service restarts.
  3. Wait for Live, then curl -sS https://<service>.onrender.com/healthz.
  4. Press Verify in the console again, and repeat the signed-curl check above. Expected: green, and 200.
  5. Update the team password note and every student's local .env — the local copy is used only by test/helpers/lineWebhook.js to sign fixtures, so a stale one shows up as an integration test failing on signature verification, days later (§5.8.3).

Rotating the channel access token

The dangerous order is the obvious one. Issue the new token first and put it into Render before revoking the old one; a revoked token with nothing to replace it turns every push into a 401, and §5.6.5 sets those rows straight to attempt_count = 3, so they will not retry themselves — that is R3, and it is avoidable here.

  1. Console → Messaging API tab → issue a new long-lived Channel access token.
  2. Render → LINE_CHANNEL_ACCESS_TOKEN → paste → Save → wait for Live.
  3. Prove the new token pushes before touching the old one: Settings → รันงานแจ้งเตือนตอนนี้, or approve one bill for a tenant whose binding is real, then
    mysql "$DATABASE_URL" -e "SELECT status, http_status, last_error FROM notifications ORDER BY id DESC LIMIT 5"
    Expected: sent with http_status 200, or the test-mode swallow row of §5.6.5 with last_error = 'test mode, no whitelisted recipient'. Anything with http_status 401 means step 2 did not take effect.
  4. Only now, revoke the old token in the console. Then update the team note.

R3. A revoked or expired LINE access token, and clearing the backlog

Trigger: messages stop arriving, delivery badges read ส่งไม่สำเร็จ, or the Settings quota line ส่งข้อความเดือนนี้: {N}/500 stops moving while bills are being approved.

Detection — the exact query

§5.6.5's error map is the diagnosis: a 401 or 403 writes status='failed' and sets attempt_count straight to 3, deliberately, because a wrong token will not fix itself and three pointless retries per row would only spend the clock. The consequence is the part that surprises people: these rows are outside the daily job's retry window forever, since the drain retries failed rows with attempt_count < 3 (§4.11.2 step 4). Fixing the token is therefore only half the recovery.

-- is it the token, and how big is the backlog?
SELECT http_status, COUNT(*) AS rows_affected,
       MIN(send_date) AS oldest, MAX(last_attempt_at) AS newest
  FROM notifications
 WHERE status = 'failed'
 GROUP BY http_status;
-- 401 or 403 in that list  =>  the token. 400 => a bad userId (a different problem,
-- and D-44 says never unbind a 'Useed…' id). 429 => the monthly allowance is gone.

Recovery

  1. Replace the token — the four steps of R2's token rotation. Do not skip its step 3: clearing a backlog against a token that still does not work re-fails every row and doubles the work.
  2. Re-arm the failed rows. This is one of only two statements in this runbook that writes to an application table, and it exists because attempt_count = 3 is a terminal state by design: 
    UPDATE notifications
   SET status = 'queued', attempt_count = 0, http_status = NULL, last_error = NULL
 WHERE status = 'failed'
   AND http_status IN (401, 403)
   AND send_date >= CURDATE() - INTERVAL 7 DAY;
    The seven-day window is not arbitrary — it is the drain's own retry window (§5.9 assumption 4), and it is what stops a fortnight-old BILL arriving at a tenant who paid a week ago. dedupe_key is a generated column and is untouched by this update, so uq_notifications_dedupe still guarantees that a message type already delivered for that bill and that day cannot go out twice. retry_key is also untouched, so LINE's own idempotency layer still holds (§5.6.4).
  3. Drain them. Settings → รันงานแจ้งเตือนตอนนี้, which runs the identical code path as daily_manual and skips the once-per-day guard (D-32). See R8 for the shell equivalent.
  4. Confirm. Re-run the detection query. Expected: no 401/403 group remains, and
    SELECT status, COUNT(*) FROM notifications WHERE send_date = CURDATE() GROUP BY status;
    shows the rows as sent. The quota line on Settings moves by the same number.
The no-SQL alternative, and when it is the right one

For a handful of rows, do not open a shell at all: Bill Detail's ส่งแจ้งเตือนอีกครั้ง (POST /bills/:id/notify) is never capped and regenerates retry_key, which is exactly what a manual resend should do (D-30, D-33, §5.6.4). It is the tool of last resort for one bill and the wrong tool for sixty-seven — hence step 2 above.

R4. Lost Admin — recovering when nobody can log in

Trigger: the only Admin password is lost, or the only Admin account was set Inactive (the login page then says บัญชีนี้ถูกปิดการใช้งาน, which is how the two cases are told apart).

There is no route that can help, and that is a decision, not an omission

D-37 refuses self-service password reset: no email, no security questions, no reset token. D-46 makes POST /users/:id/password the only reset path, and it requires an authenticated Admin. So when the last Admin is locked out, every HTTP surface of this system is correctly refusing to help, and the recovery is necessarily out of band — from the host's shell, against the database. The supported fix is the second Admin account of R1 step 6. This procedure is what you do when that account was never created.

  1. Open a shell that has DATABASE_URL. Render Shell on the deployed service, or any machine with the Aiven URI exported. Both bcryptjs and mysql2 are already runtime dependencies (§6.1.1), so nothing has to be installed.
  2. Run the reset. The password is passed in the environment, not as an argument, so it does not sit in ps output; the leading space keeps it out of shell history in zsh and bash with HISTCONTROL=ignorespace: 
     NEWPW='Recover#2026' node --input-type=module -e "
import bcrypt from 'bcryptjs';
import mysql from 'mysql2/promise';
const c = await mysql.createConnection(process.env.DATABASE_URL);
const [r] = await c.execute(
  \"UPDATE users SET password_hash = ?, status = 'Active' WHERE username = 'admin'\",
  [bcrypt.hashSync(process.env.NEWPW, 10)]);
console.log(r.affectedRows + ' row(s) updated');
await c.end();
"
    Expected output: 1 row(s) updated. 0 means the username is not admin — list them with SELECT id, username, role, status FROM users; and put the right one in the statement. Cost 10 is not negotiable: it is the cost routes/auth.js and POST /password hash at (D-37, §3.3.1), and a hash written at any other cost still verifies but silently changes the login time budget every later measurement assumes.
  3. Two-step variant, if you would rather not run JavaScript against production. Generate the hash on a laptop —
    node -e "console.log(require('bcryptjs').hashSync(process.argv[1], 10))" 'Recover#2026'
    — then paste the resulting 60-character $2b$10$… string into
    UPDATE users SET password_hash = '<hash>', status = 'Active' WHERE username = 'admin';
    The hash is not a secret and may safely cross a chat window; the password it was made from must not.
  4. Log in and immediately change it through the application at GET/POST /password, so the value you typed into a shell stops being the live password.
  5. Create the second Admin at /users before doing anything else, so this page is never needed again (D-46).
  6. Record it in the team note. This statement bypasses the application, so it writes no audit_log row — lib/audit.js is only ever called on a transaction's own connection (D-38), and inventing a synthetic actor here would put a lie in the one table whose value is that it never contains one. The note is the audit trail for exactly this event: who ran it, when, and why. Do not rotate SESSION_SECRET as part of this — it logs every user out and recovers nothing (§6.3.4).

R5. CHECK … REGEXP rejected by the database

Trigger: npm run db:migrate aborts on a CREATE TABLE with a constraint error rather than a syntax error — a managed provider on MySQL < 8.0.16, or a MariaDB-flavoured service sold as "MySQL". §2.8 records this fallback; here is how to execute it.

  1. Confirm the cause before changing anything. mysql "$DATABASE_URL" -e "SELECT VERSION()". At 8.0.16 or above, CHECK … REGEXP is legal and deterministic, and the real error is elsewhere — read the statement the migration actually failed on. Only a version below that, or a non-MySQL engine, justifies what follows.
  2. Comment out only the seven affected constraints in db/schema.sql, each with the same marker so a grep finds them all. They carry the five rules §2.8 names:
    • ck_bills_period and ck_readings_period — YYYY-MM
    • ck_readings_prev_period — YYYY-MM or the literal 'baseline'
    • ck_tenants_phone — ^0[0-9]{9}$
    • ck_receipts_no — ^REC-[0-9]{6}-[0-9]{6}$
    • ck_announcements_floors — the comma-separated digit list (keep the non-REGEXP half of this constraint, which pairs audience with audience_floors and needs no regular expression)
    • ck_link_codes_alphabet — the D-29 alphabet
    -- fallback (runbook R5): CHECK…REGEXP unsupported on this server.
-- The rule is enforced at layer 2 by lib/schemas.js — see spec §3.6.
-- CONSTRAINT ck_tenants_phone CHECK (phone REGEXP '^0[0-9]{9}$')
  3. Verify the layer-2 rule exists for each one in lib/schemas.js before you rely on it. All five are already there — that is precisely why this fallback is cheap and why it was chosen over a trigger (ADR §6.8, §3.6). A commented-out CHECK with no matching zod rule is not a fallback, it is a deleted constraint.
  4. npm run db:reset && npm test. Expected: green. The unit and integration tiers assert the Thai validation messages and the 422 envelope, not the constraint names, so nothing in the suite depends on the CHECKs being present.
  5. Write the consequence down in the PR description and in the team note: the constraint count of Appendix §C drops from twenty-one to fifteen — six constraints are removed outright and ck_announcements_floors survives in its reduced, non-REGEXP form — and for those five rules lib/schemas.js is now the only place they live — a route that skips middleware/validate.js can now write a malformed row that the database would previously have refused. That is the actual cost, and it is why the constraints are there in the first place.
What not to do instead

Do not replace a rejected CHECK with a trigger: a trigger is a second place for a rule to live, it is invisible in schema.sql's reading order, and A-3's "no migration framework" argument applies to it twice over. Do not simply delete the rule. Do not switch providers on the afternoon you discover this — the fallback takes twenty minutes and a provider migration takes a day.

R6. Backup and restore

Trigger: before every milestone review and the day before the defence (backup); a dropped table, a bad db:reset against the wrong database, or an Aiven service that has gone away (restore). §6.3.5 states the policy; this is the procedure.

What is actually being protected

Acceptable data loss is everything. All seed content is fictional (D-44) and the graded demo is rebuilt from two committed files. The recovery objective is time: a destroyed database is back to a demonstrable state in under 10 minutes (NFR-23, authored in §1.9). The dumps are not insurance against data loss — they exist so a rehearsed state, for example a receipt already numbered REC-202611-000852, can be reproduced exactly instead of re-earned by clicking.

Taking a dump

# from any machine that can reach the Aiven service, after the DBH/DBP/… split above
mysqldump --single-transaction --set-gtid-purged=OFF \
          --default-character-set=utf8mb4 \
          -h"$DBH" -P"$DBP" -u"$DBU" -p"$DBW" --ssl-mode=REQUIRED \
          "$DBN" \
  | gzip > db/dumps/mysqldump-M4-$(date +%F).sql.gz

ls -lh db/dumps/          # a dump under ~200 KB gzipped is empty — check before trusting it

--single-transaction keeps the dump consistent without locking the office out mid-payment; --default-character-set=utf8mb4 is what stops Thai names coming back as question marks. db/dumps/*.sql.gz is git-ignored (§6.1.1), so a dump lives on the laptop that took it and in the team's shared drive — never in the repository.

Restoring — the fast path, and the exact one

  1. Rebuild from source. This is the real backup and the 10-minute path. 
    mysql "$DATABASE_URL" -e "DROP DATABASE IF EXISTS juristic;
    CREATE DATABASE juristic CHARACTER SET utf8mb4 COLLATE utf8mb4_0900_ai_ci"
npm run db:reset      # schema.sql + seed.sql, about 20 seconds
npm run rehearse      # asserts the PRD §28 arithmetic end to end
    Expected: 80 rooms · 67 tenants · 54 LINE-linked · rates 18.00/8.00/35.00 · due day 5, then the rehearsal line ending total 1,790.00 and exit code 0. Measured end to end — creating the database, resetting, rehearsing — this is about two minutes; the remaining eight in the budget are for finding the credentials, which is the part that actually takes the time and the reason the team note matters more than the dumps do.
  2. Restore a specific dump, when the rehearsed state itself is what is wanted: 
    gunzip -c db/dumps/mysqldump-M4-2026-11-01.sql.gz \
  | mysql --default-character-set=utf8mb4 \
          -h"$DBH" -P"$DBP" -u"$DBU" -p"$DBW" --ssl-mode=REQUIRED "$DBN"
    Expected: silence, then 
    mysql "$DATABASE_URL" -e "SELECT
  (SELECT COUNT(*) FROM rooms)    AS rooms,
  (SELECT COUNT(*) FROM bills)    AS bills,
  (SELECT seq FROM counters WHERE name='receipt') AS receipt_seq"
    reading 80 rooms and a receipt counter at or above 851.
  3. Prove a dump is restorable before you need it. Once, on a laptop: restore the newest dump into a scratch database named juristic_restore_test and run the count query above. A dump nobody has ever restored is not a backup. Do this the day before the defence, as part of preparing the offline laptop of R7.
The one destructive mistake this procedure invites

npm run db:reset aimed at a DATABASE_URL you did not read is how a rehearsed demo database is lost. Check which database the shell is pointed at first — mysql "$DATABASE_URL" -e "SELECT DATABASE(), @@hostname" — and remember that .env.test must never point at juristic (§6.2.3). To re-arm room 205 without discarding the other 66 approved bills, the command is npm run db:demo-arm, not db:reset (§2.7, §6.2.5).

R7. Demo-day fallback

Trigger: the morning of a milestone review or the defence. Everything here is preparation done before anyone is in the room, plus three failures and what to say when each one happens.

The morning checklist

  1. Wake the instance. curl -sS https://<service>.onrender.com/healthz five minutes before the slot, and again just before walking in. The first request to a sleeping free instance takes about 50 seconds, and it is the worst possible first impression for a system that is otherwise fast.
  2. Run §5.8.5 เตรียมสาธิต LINE in full. On an untouched seed neither mode delivers a message to a phone — test mode ON swallows it (§5.6.5), test mode OFF pushes at a Useed… id and gets a 400. Five minutes, and it cannot be recovered in the room.
  3. npm run db:demo-arm — room 205 back to unread and unbilled, receipt counter back to 851, the other 66 bills untouched (§2.7).
  4. npm run rehearse — the whole path, asserting 1,790.00 and never a date. If it is red, the demo is red; fix that before anything else on this list.
  5. Take a dump (R6) and restore it onto one laptop's local MySQL, then run npm run rehearse there too. That laptop is the fallback, and an untested fallback is not one.
  6. Print one receipt and one invoice on paper from the A5 print views. A printer that is discovered to be broken during the demo costs the print half of D-28; a sheet already in hand costs nothing.

If the internet is down

  1. Run the app locally against local MySQL restored from seed.sql — the DEV branch of §6.3.1's topology, which exists for exactly this. npm run db:reset && npm run dev, and the whole PRD §28 path works: readings, generation, approval, payment, receipt number, both print views, every report.
  2. Say the two things that will not work, before an examiner finds them. The Dashboard's four Chart.js charts are blank, because the library is loaded from cdnjs.cloudflare.com and the CSP names no local copy (§6.1.1); and the Thai text renders in a system fallback face rather than Noto Sans Thai, because the stylesheet comes from Google Fonts. Both are stated trade-offs of a zero-build, zero-asset repository, and the reports — which are HTML tables and CSV, not charts — carry the same numbers the charts would have.
  3. The webhook is unreachable, so no linking demo offline. Show a tenant that is already bound in the seed instead, and the notification rows behind the delivery badges.

If LINE is down, the token is dead, or the allowance is gone

  1. Leave โหมดทดสอบ LINE ON. The swallow branch of §5.6.5 records the row as sent with last_error = 'test mode, no whitelisted recipient', so the badge reads ส่งแล้ว and every screen, counter and report shows the demo state exactly as it would in a real send.
  2. Demonstrate delivery from Bill Detail's timeline and the notifications rows rather than from a phone. That is the system's own record of delivery, it is what the Admin screens read, and it is honest.
  3. Do not switch test mode off to "try harder". One bulk approve at 67 rooms spends most of a month's 500-message allowance (§5.6.2, R-02), and there is no second pool.

If the data looks wrong in the room

npm run db:demo-arm — one command, idempotent, safe to run twice, and it leaves the other 66 approved bills alone. Never db:reset during a demo: it is a 20-second rebuild that discards everything else on the instance, including any rehearsed payment an examiner has just watched you take.

R8. Triggering the daily job by hand, and reading job_runs

Trigger: a rehearsal at any hour, a demo of the reminder rule, clearing a backlog after R3, or diagnosing a morning where nothing was sent.

The two ways to run it

PathHowjob_nameOnce-per-day guard
Settings button — the one to useรันงานแจ้งเตือนตอนนี้ → POST /settings/run-daily-job, Admin sessiondaily_manualSkipped, deliberately — a 14:00 rehearsal still works after the 09:00 cron ran (D-32)
Machine endpoint — what cron-job.org callsPOST /internal/jobs/daily with X-Job-Token, no session, no CSRFdailyEnforced by uq_job_runs_name_date — a second call the same day returns already_ran: true and does nothing
 
curl -sS -X POST https://<service>.onrender.com/internal/jobs/daily \
     -H "X-Job-Token: $JOB_TOKEN"

{"data":{"job":"daily","run_date":"2026-09-07","already_ran":false,
         "pre_due":3,"overdue":5,"retried":0,"sent":8,"failed":0,"skipped_no_line":2}}

The Settings button reports the same run in Thai: ส่งแจ้งเตือนก่อนครบกำหนด {a} ราย · เลยกำหนด {b} ราย · ส่งซ้ำที่ล้มเหลว {c} ราย. Neither path ever writes a bill status (§4.11.2) — the job queues and drains messages, and nothing else.

What to check in job_runs

SELECT id, job_name, run_date, started_at, finished_at, summary_json
  FROM job_runs
 ORDER BY id DESC
 LIMIT 7;
What you seeWhat it meansWhat to do
One daily row per date, finished_at set, summary_json presentHealthy. This is the whole monitoring story for the scheduler (§6.3.6)Nothing
No row at all for a dateThe cron never authenticated — X-Job-Token did not match, so the request was rejected before any row was written. Nothing warns you: cron-job.org sees a 401, and the app sees an unauthenticated requestCompare JOB_TOKEN in Render against the header in the cron-job.org job definition, character for character. Then re-run by hand
started_at set, finished_at NULLThe run began and died mid-drain — the free instance slept, or the database connection droppedSafe to re-run with the Settings button. uq_notifications_dedupe guarantees no tenant gets the same message type for the same bill twice on one day (ENG-3)
already_ran: true from the machine endpointNormal, not an error — a retrying scheduler hitting the guard (D-32)Nothing. If you actually need a second run, use the Settings button; that is what daily_manual exists for
Row present, every count 0The job ran and nothing was eligibleCheck with the eligibility predicate of §4.11.2 — reminders fire only at due − 2 and due + 1 / 8 / 15, and a Paid or voided-payment bill is excluded (D-25, D-33)
failed non-zero in summary_jsonDelivery, not schedulingR3's detection query tells you whether it is the token, a bad userId or the monthly allowance (§5.6.5)
The mistake worth naming

Calling POST /internal/jobs/daily to "test" the job after the 09:00 cron has already run returns already_ran: true and does nothing at all — which reads exactly like a broken job. The Settings button is not a convenience wrapper around that endpoint; it is a second entry point that deliberately skips the guard, and it is the one to reach for at any hour that is not 09:00.

C. Editorial reconciliations applied when this document was assembled

Six sections were authored in parallel against the same ADR. Where two of them named the same thing differently, the conflict was resolved once, here, in favour of ADR-001 first and the data model second; the surviving name is used everywhere in this document.

This count supersedes ADR §6.8's "eleven unique keys", which predates uq_rooms(room_code) and uq_users(username); the ADR is not contradicted, it is out of date by two keys, and §3.6 lists all thirteen by nameThe DDL in §2.4, counted

Open items — carried, not resolved
  • Money width. The section brief for the data model asked for DECIMAL(12,2); D-16 and ADR §6.4 say DECIMAL(10,2), and that is what ships. Recorded in §2 rather than silently applied.
  • D-42's two halves. "Every Unpaid bill across all periods" and "the four bucket counts sum to the row count" cannot both hold while a not-yet-due bill is a row. The arrears report's rows are the bills already past due; the residual is a summary line above the table and the all-time figure stays on the Dashboard (§4.13.3).
  • LINE template placeholders keep the elec_ prefix ({elec_units}, {elec_rate}, {elec_amount}) because D-31 fixes the message text; payload keys and SQL aliases use electric_. The two namespaces never meet in one expression.
